| ---------------- | -------------------------------------------------------- |
| `common.rs`      | Base config, routes, outline printer.                    |
| `compile.rs`     | Default and custom chains, step keys, compile errors.    |
| `maps.rs`        | Stale entries, error maps, limits, wire nodes, skeleton. |
| `auth.rs`        | Built-in login and JWKS routes.                          |
| `collections.rs` | Collection routes.                                       |

| Test                                                              | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `default_chains_follow_the_spec_order`                            | Default chain order (proc, callees, cache for reads, databases), one op-free tree for GET and POST, and default bodies shared through `Arc`.                                                                            |
| `static_endpoint_runs_the_gateway_chain`                          | A static endpoint (override, no owner) runs the gateway's `proc` and auth call and keeps its override.                                                                                                                  |
| `custom_chain_starts_with_the_owners_work`                        | A custom `chain:` keeps the owner's `proc` first.                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `call_bodies_inline_with_and_via`                                 | Inline `chain`, `with` (named chain) and `via` (another endpoint's body and op) resolve; step `op` shows on the call.                                                                                                   |
| `head_routes_copy_get_with_their_own_key`                         | A `GET` gets a synthesized `HEAD` sharing its chain; an explicit `HEAD` entry keeps `GET`'s owner and op.                                                                                                               |
| `step_keys_count_occurrences_per_target_across_parallel_branches` | Occurrence counts per target, with `parallel` transparent: `db#0 cache#0 db#1 db#2 orders#0`.                                                                                                                           |
| `errors_name_the_endpoint_and_point_at_the_step`                  | Every compile error (step, field, entry key, node chain, edge `calls`) names the endpoint and points at the exact `line:col`.                                                                                           |
| `static_routes_need_a_gateway_and_stale_entries_warn`             | A stale entry warns and is dropped; a static route without a gateway is an error at its key.                                                                                                                            |
| `via_target_error_map_is_ignored_with_a_warning`                  | A `via:` target's `error_map` is ignored with a warning; keys are stored lowercase.                                                                                                                                     |
| `limits_are_named_errors`                                         | 256 steps, 32 branches, 16 nesting levels, resolved hops (100 000) and call depth (64): each error names its limit, and the exact cap is accepted.                                                                      |
| `resolve_error_looks_up_node_then_status_then_class`              | `error_map` lookup order node, status, class; no match keeps the failure's own status.                                                                                                                                  |
| `deep_call_chains_drop_without_deep_recursion`                    | A 5000-service chain listed leaf-first compiles (to its depth error) and drops on a 256 KiB stack: `Seq` frees nested bodies with a work list (R6.12).                                                                  |
| `error_map_keys_are_strict_and_unique`                            | `error_map` keys `+503`, `0503`, `503`, `50`, `6xx` are rejected, and `5xx`/`5XX` are reported as the same key instead of collapsing.                                                                                   |
| `head_entry_of_a_static_get_warns_that_the_copy_is_served`        | A `HEAD` entry next to a static `GET` entry warns it is ignored; the synthesized copy of the `GET` is served.                                                                                                           |
| `via_target_override_is_ignored_with_a_warning`                   | `via:` runs the target's chain, not its `override`, and warns.                                                                                                                                                          |
| `an_edge_with_calls_is_one_cache_step_with_a_repeat`              | An edge `calls: 3` to a cache is one default-chain `CacheStep` with `repeat: 3`, not three steps.                                                                                                                       |
| `skeleton_lists_missing_spec_routes_sorted`                       | `skeleton` lists only the spec routes with no entry, sorted, one `"KEY": {} # owner NODE, op OP` line each; existing keys match after normalizing method case and path spaces.                                          |
| `login_and_jwks_are_built_in_with_the_auth_edge`                  | With a gateway auth edge, `POST /login` and the JWKS are auth-owned read routes with their `Responder`; the login runs inside the gateway's auth call (auth once), ordinary routes keep the `proc`-only call.           |
| `login_without_the_auth_edge_runs_auth_inline`                    | Without the edge the login body follows the gateway's `proc` inline; the JWKS executes as exactly gateway proc then auth proc (no network hop).                                                                         |
| `gatewayless_and_auth_only_logins_start_at_the_auth_node`         | No gateway: the chain is the body alone; an auth node alone compiles both routes and the JWKS HEAD copy.                                                                                                                |
| `login_charges_auth_once_even_with_edge_calls`                    | An edge `calls: 2` doubles the ordinary routes' auth call but not the login or JWKS.                                                                                                                                    |
| `a_login_chain_runs_with_the_auth_node_as_runner`                 | A custom `chain:` on either built-in route runs from the auth node; a step auth has no edge for is a placed compile error.                                                                                              |
| `a_spec_route_replaces_the_built_in_route_with_a_warning`         | A spec `POST /login` wins, drops the responder and warns; the JWKS stays built in.                                                                                                                                      |
| `endpoint_entries_customise_built_in_routes`                      | An `endpoints:` entry gives the login an `error_map` and the JWKS an `override` without being stale; the responder stays.                                                                                               |
| `head_copy_of_jwks_keeps_its_responder`                           | The synthesized `HEAD` of the JWKS keeps its responder and chain; `POST /login` gets no HEAD copy.                                                                                                                      |
| `a_head_entry_for_the_jwks_is_a_copy_of_the_built_in_route`       | A `HEAD /.well-known/jwks.json` entry with an `override`, with or without a gateway, is the auth node's read route with the `Jwks` responder and its own override, not a static route (and no error without a gateway). |
| `without_an_auth_node_there_are_no_built_in_routes`               | No auth node, no built-in routes; a `POST /login` entry is stale and warns.                                                                                                                                             |
| `error_map_resolves_node_then_kind_then_status_then_class`        | `resolve_error` order is node, kind (`reset`, `malformed`, `timeout`), exact status, class (Z8); a reset or malformed with no matching key answers 502, a timeout 504.                                                  |
| `error_map_accepts_the_kind_keys`                                 | `reset`, `malformed` and `timeout` (any case) are valid `error_map` keys at load.                                                                                                                                       |
| `wire_nodes_are_the_gateway_and_the_owner`                        | `Endpoint::is_wire_node` is true for the gateway and the owner only; `entry_node` is the gateway, else the owner.                                                                                                       |
| `an_edge_prob_is_the_default_call_prob`                           | (`prob.rs`) An edge `prob` becomes `Call::prob` of the default-chain call over it.                                                                                                                                      |
| `no_prob_means_one`                                               | Without a `prob` the compiled call has 1.0.                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `a_step_prob_overrides_the_edge`                                  | A step's own `prob` wins over the edge's.                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `a_step_prob_out_of_range_is_a_positioned_error`                  | A step `prob` of 0, 1.2 or -1 fails at the step (line 23) with `prob must be above 0`.                                                                                                                                  |
| `a_cache_timeout_below_the_hit_p50_warns`                         | (`compile.rs`) A cache with `hit: 5ms, timeout: 2ms` (and a `p50: 5ms` pair with `timeout: 4ms`) gives one compile warning naming the cache and "cut to misses".                                                        |
| `a_cache_timeout_at_or_above_the_hit_p50_or_none_is_quiet`        | `timeout` of 20 ms, equal to the hit cost, or none: no warning.                                                                                                                                                         |

## `crates/kaanal-core/tests/splice.rs` — edits that keep the rest of the text

See [splice](files/kaanal-core/splice.md).

| Test                                                                     | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `set_replaces_only_the_value_span`                                       | A scalar, a quoted scalar, a flow map and a list item are replaced and nothing else changes.                                                                                                                                   |
| `set_on_an_empty_value_and_on_a_block_collection`                        | `key:` with no value and a block mapping are set after their key's colon.                                                                                                                                                      |
| `insert_into_a_flow_map_keeps_the_style`                                 | `, key: value` goes after the last entry; an empty `{ }` or `{}` becomes `{ key: value }`.                                                                                                                                     |
| `insert_into_a_block_map_matches_the_indent`                             | A new line after the last entry with the same indent (nested, at the root before a trailing comment, and in a map that starts on a `-` line).                                                                                  |
| `insert_into_an_empty_document_and_without_a_final_newline`              | An empty or comment-only document gets its first key; a file with no final newline gets a line break first.                                                                                                                    |
| `comments_and_blank_lines_survive_an_edit`                               | Three edits leave every comment, the blank line and the end-of-file comment where they were.                                                                                                                                   |
| `crlf_and_bom_are_kept`                                                  | The BOM stays and every new line ending is CRLF in a CRLF file.                                                                                                                                                                |
| `non_ascii_text_before_an_edit_does_not_shift_it`                        | Characters outside ASCII before an edit do not move it (character index versus byte offset).                                                                                                                                   |
| `an_anchor_or_alias_target_is_refused_with_a_lost_entry`                 | An anchored value and an alias are left alone and listed; the rest of the document is still editable.                                                                                                                          |
| `remove_takes_the_whole_entry_and_reports_the_comment_left_behind`       | Block entries go with their line, a comment above stays and is listed, flow entries (first, middle, last, only) and list items (block and flow) go cleanly.                                                                    |
| `a_missing_path_or_a_duplicate_key_is_an_error_and_edits_apply_in_order` | `NotFound`, `Exists` and `Parse` errors; a later edit sees the result of an earlier one.                                                                                                                                       |
| `removing_a_block_scalar_or_an_empty_value_does_not_take_the_next_entry` | `                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `inserting_after_a_block_scalar_or_empty_last_value_stays_in_its_parent` | A key inserted after such a last value lands in its own mapping, not after the next top-level entry.                                                                                                                           |
| `setting_a_block_scalar_keeps_the_next_key_on_its_own_line`              | Setting a block scalar replaces header and text; the next key is not joined to it and the result parses.                                                                                                                       |
| `editing_inside_an_anchored_value_is_refused_not_applied`                | A set, insert or remove inside an anchored map (flow and block) changes nothing and is listed; a sibling is still editable; an alias is not edited.                                                                            |
| `quoted_keys_are_found_by_their_value`                                   | `"a"` and `'b c'` are found by `a` and `b c`; inserting `b c` is `Exists`.                                                                                                                                                     |
| `an_inserted_key_can_never_carry_syntax`                                 | A key with a colon and a newline is quoted and stays one key; `yes` and `404` are quoted, `ok_key-2` is bare; flow maps quote too.                                                                                             |
| `duplicate_keys_are_a_parse_error_not_a_silent_first_match`              | `a: 1\na: 2` is `Parse("duplicate key 'a'")`.                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `what_an_edit_cannot_keep_is_listed`                                     | A comment between key and block value, the only child of a mapping, and a root edit each add a `lost` line.                                                                                                                    |
| `a_key_is_renamed_in_place_and_nothing_else_moves`                       | `RenameKey` changes only the key's bytes (comments and spacing stay), handles a quoted key and a new name that needs quotes, and refuses a taken name (`Exists`), a missing key, a list item and a key inside an anchored map. |
| `unedited_bytes_never_change`                                            | Setting every scalar leaf of three documents (including the demo fixture) to its own text reproduces the file byte for byte.                                                                                                   |
| `a_unit_suffix_survives_edits_to_other_fields`                           | (F7) A file with `1MB/s`, `250 KB/s`, `250KB` and `2KB` keeps those lines byte for byte when `proc` and `rps` are set; setting the field itself writes the integer given.                                                      |

## `crates/kaanal-core/tests/chaos_remap.rs` — chaos by name across topologies

See [chaos](files/kaanal-core/chaos.md).

| Test                                                               | What it proves                                                                                                                                                  |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `keys_move_to_their_new_ids_and_carry_their_layer`                 | Node, edge, endpoint and node-at-endpoint keys land on their new ids with their layer intact; the old id is not left behind; only the removed node is reported. |
| `a_key_whose_node_or_endpoint_is_gone_is_dropped_and_reported`     | A key naming a removed node, edge or endpoint is dropped and listed, whatever its other half.                                                                   |
| `a_replica_beyond_the_new_count_is_dropped_and_one_within_is_kept` | A replica key survives only while the new node still has that replica.                                                                                          |
| `an_identity_map_changes_nothing`                                  | Mapping every id to itself returns an equal state and drops nothing.                                                                                            |

## `crates/kaanal-core/tests/chaos.rs` — layers, compile and banding

| Test                                                                                   | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `resolve_merges_node_then_endpoint_scoped_node_most_specific_last`                     | `compile().visit(node, endpoint)` folds the node layer and the node-at-endpoint layer (latencies sum); a node with no entry has none (it used to be a zero `NodeChaos`).                                                                                        |
| `node_at_goes_before_node_in_a_layer_and_the_endpoint_layer_is_left_to_the_front_door` | Within one layer the node-at fault takes the first band, then the node's; the endpoint-wide entry is not in `visit`, only in `EffectiveChaos::endpoint` (kind-only layers no longer carry a kind, so each layer sets rate 1.0).                                 |
| `remove_deletes_exactly_the_named_key`                                                 | `remove` deletes only the key named (chaos on `GET /x` never reaches `HEAD /x`); checked through `compile().visit(..).down`.                                                                                                                                    |
| `validate_checks_only_the_fields_that_are_set`                                         | `ChaosLayer::validate` checks set fields only, including `error_status` 500-504 for every kind.                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `old_collect_and_insert_still_work_and_first_error_is_deterministic`                   | The `FromIterator`/`insert` API keeps working; `first_error` names the same entry every run.                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `first_error_applies_the_link_key_rules`                                               | The simulator's check rejects jitter on a node key and a status error on an edge, and accepts jitter on an edge.                                                                                                                                                |
| `error_kind_parses_its_names`                                                          | `ErrorKind::parse` accepts `status                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `dense_fills_defaults_for_one_layer`                                                   | `ChaosLayer::dense`: unset fields show their defaults; a set rate comes back bit for bit.                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `fail_kind_names`                                                                      | `FailKind::as_str` names.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `entries_are_sorted_nodes_then_endpoints_then_node_at`                                 | `entries()` order: nodes by id, endpoints by name, node-at by (id, name).                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `one_fault_is_bit_identical_to_the_old_rule`                                           | A single 30% status fault compiles to one band whose `upto` is exactly 0.3 (not 0.30000000000000004); roll 0.29 hits (status 503) and roll 0.3 does not, as in B2a (Z15).                                                                                       |
| `mixed_faults_are_banded_top_layer_first`                                              | Two faults (30% reset above 30% 503) band on one roll: about 30% reset and 21% 503, the top layer's kind first (S8).                                                                                                                                            |
| `layer_order_beats_specificity`                                                        | A higher layer's node `hang` beats a lower layer's node@endpoint `reset` on the same visit (roll 0.5 lands on `hang`): the layer decides, not the key (S6).                                                                                                     |
| `same_layer_tie_goes_to_the_more_specific_key`                                         | In one layer a node@endpoint `reset` is ahead of the node's `hang` for that endpoint; another endpoint sees the node's `hang`.                                                                                                                                  |
| `kind_and_status_are_one_unit`                                                         | A lower layer (50% hang 504) under a top layer that sets only rate 50% and status 500: roll 0.1 gives `status` 500 from the top, roll 0.6 gives the lower `hang`; no hang+500 hybrid exists (S7).                                                               |
| `override_drops_lower_layers_for_that_key_only`                                        | A top `replace` calm on node 0 removes the lower layer's `down` and 100% reset on that node (roll 0.0 no longer hits); node 1's lower `down` is untouched (S13).                                                                                                |
| `latency_is_clamped_after_summing_everywhere`                                          | Node, endpoint and node@endpoint entries of 240 s in each of two layers: both `visit` and `endpoint` clamp the sum to `MAX_LATENCY_ADD` (S9).                                                                                                                   |
| `faults_past_the_cap_fold_into_the_last_band`                                          | More than `MAX_FAULTS` layers: the extra faults fold into the last band by rate union, which keeps its own kind.                                                                                                                                                |
| `compile_matches_a_slow_reference_over_random_stacks`                                  | Property test: up to 12 random layers over a node, a node-at-endpoint pair and an endpoint (rates, kinds, latency past the clamp, `down`, `replace`, kind without a rate) equal a slow reference for both `visit` and `endpoint`; stacks pass the 8-band cap.   |
| `empty_state_is_empty`                                                                 | An empty `ChaosState` compiles to an `EffectiveChaos` whose `is_empty` is true (the executor's fast path).                                                                                                                                                      |
| `node_override_calms_the_nodes_pairs_from_lower_layers`                                | A node-key override also drops that node's lower node@endpoint entries; a pair set in the same or a higher layer still applies.                                                                                                                                 |
| `pair_override_leaves_node_level_entries`                                              | A node@endpoint override affects only its pair; the node's own entries stay.                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `endpoint_override_only_touches_that_endpoint`                                         | An endpoint-key `replace` calm clears `GET /x` (not down) while `GET /y` stays down and the node-level visit of `GET /x` stays down.                                                                                                                            |
| `override_also_drops_lower_latency`                                                    | A top `replace` layer with 5 ms over a lower 2 s on the same node compiles to exactly 5 ms.                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `a_kind_without_a_rate_adds_no_fault_and_warns`                                        | Node 30% 503 plus a node@endpoint layer with only `error_kind: hang`: one fault, roll 0.2 hits 503, roll 0.35 misses; `ChaosState::warnings` is exactly the "have no effect without error_rate" line for node 0 at `GET /x`, and gives none once a rate is set. |

## `crates/kaanal-core/tests/scenario.rs` — names, set and stack

| Test                                                      | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| --------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `names_follow_the_grammar`                                | `valid_name` accepts `a`, `payments-down`, `x_1`, `0abc` and refuses empty, `A`, `Payments`, a leading `-` or `_`, spaces, `/`, `\`, `:`, `..`, `x.yaml`, 65 characters, the reserved words `active`/`save`/`reset` and the device names `con`, `NUL`, `com1`, `lpt9`. |
| `endpoint_key_upper_cases_the_method`                     | `endpoint_key("get /users/{id}")` gives `GET /users/{id}`; `"GET"` (no path) and `"/x"` (no method) are errors.                                                                                                                                                        |
| `activate_stacks_in_order_and_reactivate_moves_to_top`    | Activation order is the stack order; re-activating `a` gives `[b, a]`; `deactivate` returns true then false; activating an unknown name is an error (S4).                                                                                                              |
| `live_is_always_on_top_and_deactivate_restores`           | The live layer beats every scenario; deactivating restores the lower result.                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `set_active_is_atomic`                                    | `set_active` with an unknown name is an error and leaves the stack as it was; an empty list empties it.                                                                                                                                                                |
| `set_rules`                                               | A name clash across sources is refused; an inline scenario cannot be undefined at runtime, a runtime one can; a bad name is refused.                                                                                                                                   |
| `name_edges`                                              | Name edge cases: 64 vs 65 characters, `prn aux nul com9 lpt1` refused, `com0 com10 lpt0 comx` accepted, `con.txt` and non-ASCII refused.                                                                                                                               |
| `endpoint_key_edges`                                      | A double space and a trailing space normalise (`get  /x`, `get /x` -> `GET /x`); `"GET"` fails with exactly `invalid endpoint 'GET': expected 'METHOD /path'`.                                                                                                         |
| `replace_rules`                                           | `replace` keeps a runtime scenario's position, refuses an inline or file one (naming the old source), and inserts an unknown name through the usual checks; `remove` of an unknown name errors.                                                                        |
| `replace_of_a_new_name_obeys_the_bound`                   | A runtime `replace` of a new name past 128 scenarios is refused.                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `messages_name_the_sources`                               | Exact texts: inline vs file is "defined in both the config and a scenario file", runtime vs runtime is "already defined in the control API", an unknown `remove` is "unknown scenario 'ghost'", and removing an inline one mentions "the config".                      |
| `entries_are_bounded`                                     | 256 entries are accepted, 257 refused on `insert` and on `replace` (of an existing and of a new name), leaving only `ok`; `entry_count` counts a node, an endpoint and two node@endpoint pairs as 4.                                                                   |
| `stale_active_names_are_harmless_and_prune_clears_them`   | After `ScenarioSet::remove`, `compile` skips the stale name and `prune` clears it.                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `set_active_dedups_and_failure_leaves_a_deep_stack_alone` | `[a,b,a]` becomes `[b,a]`; a failed `set_active` leaves a three-deep stack unchanged.                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `reactivation_changes_the_winner`                         | Re-activating the lower of two conflicting scenarios flips the compiled winner.                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `bounds`                                                  | The 129th scenario (`MAX_SCENARIOS` + 1) is refused.                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |

## `crates/kaanal-core/tests/scenario_config.rs` — resolving scenarios and parsing files

| Test                                                     | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| -------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `a_scenario_resolves_to_layers`                          | A scenario with a node entry, a lowercase-method endpoint entry and a node@endpoint entry resolves with no warnings, keeps its description, counts 3 entries, and compiles to db `down` at `GET /users/{id}` and 800 ms on that endpoint.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `bad_scenarios_are_rejected_with_the_entry_and_field`    | Thirteen bad definitions each fail with a message containing the key words and the exact path (e.g. `chaos.0.node`): no target, unknown node, unknown endpoint, endpoint without `METHOD /path`, nothing set, empty `chaos`, `error_status` outside 500-504 (with a status kind or a reset kind), bad `error_kind`, rate 2, 400 000 ms latency ("5 min"), a duplicate key (`chaos.1`, "same key"), an empty `chaos: []` ("at least one of chaos or pins") and `limits:` ("not supported yet"); `pins:` is no longer reserved. |
| `too_many_raw_entries_are_refused_even_if_keys_collapse` | 257 identical entries (one key) are refused with "at most 256" before duplicate keys could collapse them.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `unreachable_pair_and_orphan_fields_are_warnings`        | A db@`GET /health` pair (a static gateway-only route) warns "never visits" and a bare `error_kind` warns about `error_rate`: exactly two warnings, load succeeds.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `a_replica_entry_resolves_to_a_replica_layer`            | (`scenario_config.rs`) `{ node: db, replica: 1, down: true }` and `{ node: db, replica: 2, latency_ms: 50 }` resolve to two replica layers; `replica_down` and `visit_replica` read them.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `bad_replica_entries_are_rejected_at_their_field`        | Replica 3 of 3, a replica without a node, and a replica with an endpoint are rejected at the `replica` field with the range or the reason.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `scenario_files_parse_and_reject_unknown_keys`           | `parse_scenario` reads a file with a BOM; an unknown key `bogus` is an error with a line; a file with only `description:` parses (`chaos` is optional; the shape check, not the parser, wants one of `chaos` or `pins`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `config_scenarios_key_parses`                            | `scenarios:` in `kaanal.yaml` parses into `Config.scenarios`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `duplicate_key_names_the_earlier_entry`                  | With a different key between them, the repeated db entry fails with "same key as chaos[0]".                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `null_limits_and_pins_read_as_absent`                    | A scenario with `limits: null` and `pins: ~` resolves without error (`resolve_scenario` now also takes an `empty_ok` callback; every test here passes an always-true `empty_ok`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |

## `crates/kaanal-core/tests/pins/` — pins, layers and the pin delay

One test target (`cargo test -p kaanal-core --test pins`), one module per topic in `tests/pins/` (R3.8):

| Module      | What it covers                             |
| ----------- | ------------------------------------------ |
| `common.rs` | Base config and builders.                  |
| `layers.rs` | Resolution, layering, validation, loading. |
| `delay.rs`  | The pin delay and its sweep.               |

The pin model in [source](files/kaanal-core/pin.md): cycle order, ranges, precedence, layering, scenario resolution, `states:`, and the `pin_delay` in the plan ([exec](files/kaanal-core/exec.md)).

| Test                                                            | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| --------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `next_cycles_and_skips_disallowed`                              | `PinState::next` from `None` walks `loading, empty, error, slow, success` and wraps to `loading`; with `empty` disallowed the cycle is four states; `parse` is exact (`Slow` is not a state) and `name` round-trips.                                                                                                                                                         |
| `validate_entry_table`                                          | `success` takes no status or delay; `error` takes 400-599 (399 and 600 fail), `empty` 200-299, `loading`/`slow` no status; a delay of 1 ms and 300 s pass for every other state, zero and 300 s + 1 ms fail.                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `resolve_precedence_and_defaults`                               | With no `states:`, `loading` resolves to 10 s, `slow` 2 s, `empty` and `success` 0, `error` to status 500; a `states.error` of 502/body/3 s wins over the defaults, and a pin entry of 429/1 s wins over `states:` while the body still comes from `states:`.                                                                                                                |
| `fold_higher_layer_wins_whole`                                  | Scenarios `a` then `b` both pin `GET /x`: `b` wins (source `Scenario("b")`) and `GET /y` is kept; re-activating `a` moves it to the top and flips the winner.                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `success_masks_a_lower_pin`                                     | A live `success` pin over an active scenario's `error` pin is the pin in force, with source `Live`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `live_beats_scenarios`                                          | A live `slow` beats a scenario's `empty`; removing the live pin makes the scenario's pin win again.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `clear_all_clears_live_pins`                                    | `ChaosStack::clear_all` empties the live pin layer.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `scenario_with_only_pins_loads`                                 | A scenario with one pin (lower-case method in the endpoint) resolves with no warnings, no chaos entries and the pin's state, status and delay under the normalized key.                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `scenario_with_neither_is_an_error`                             | `description` only, `chaos: []` with `pins: []`, and `pins: null` each fail with "at least one of chaos or pins".                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `pin_range_errors_are_placed_at_the_field`                      | A 200 status on `error`, a zero delay and an unparseable endpoint fail with the path pointing at `pins`, the index and the `status`, `delay` or `endpoint` field.                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `duplicate_pin_endpoint_is_rejected_with_both_indexes`          | The same endpoint written twice (different method case) fails naming `pins[2]` and `pins[0]`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `entries_count_chaos_and_pins`                                  | 200 chaos entries and 100 pins exceed the 256-entry bound together ("at most 256").                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `pin_on_synthesized_head_and_on_no_entry_endpoint_rejected`     | A pin on `HEAD /users/{id}` fails with "pin the GET route"; with no gateway the owning service is the entry node, so pinning that route works.                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `empty_pin_rejected_when_empty_ok_false`                        | With `empty_ok` returning false an `empty` pin fails with "no empty form for ..." and the `states.empty.body` hint; with true it loads; an `error` pin never asks.                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `pins_in_a_file_scenario_parse_with_positions`                  | `parse_scenario` reads `delay: 1500ms`; a misspelled state and an unknown field in a pin are errors on line 2; a file with `limits: {}` still fails `check_supported`.                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `endpoint_states_parse_and_reject_success_and_unknown_keys`     | `states:` with an `empty` body and an `error` of 502/2 s lands on the compiled endpoint (a `HEAD` copy carries it, other endpoints have none); `success`, an unknown key, an `error` status 200 and a 6 min delay and a `body` on `loading` or `slow` ("a loading override takes no body") are compile errors naming the problem.                                            |
| `pin_delay_zero_plan_is_identical`                              | Over three endpoints, with and without front-door chaos (hang, latency) and node chaos, 40 seeds x 5 counters: the plan with a 3 s pin delay is the zero-delay plan shifted by 3 s behind one leading hop, the planned failure moves by 3 s, a hang is actually reached, and a zero delay adds no `Delay` hop and equals `execute`'s plan (a pin adds no draw).              |
| `pin_delay_shifts_everything_without_new_draws`                 | The first plan item is a `Delay` hop on the entry node from 0 for the pin delay, and everything after it equals the unpinned plan shifted by that delay, for 20 seeds.                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `pin_delay_shifts_the_chain_and_stays_outside_the_timeout_span` | Under a gateway `timeout: 5s` a 10 s pin delay comes before the root span, which opens at 10 s; `execute` ends `Ok` with no deadline failure, the chain starts at 10 s and the total is between 10 s and 11 s.                                                                                                                                                               |
| `pin_delay_survives_endpoint_failure_and_hang`                  | Front-door chaos failing with 503 fails at exactly the pin delay (total 10 s); a front-door hang starts after the delay and lasts the 5 s gateway timeout, failing 504 at 15 s.                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `endpoint_latency_hop_starts_after_the_pin_delay`               | With endpoint `latency_add` of 50 ms and a 2 s pin delay the two `Delay` hops are (0, 2 s) and (2 s, 50 ms), and the first chain hop starts at 2.05 s.                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `pin_delay_sweep_covers_spans_forks_and_cache`                  | On a gateway/cache/`parallel`/timeout fixture, 3 chaos setups x 60 seeds x 5 counters: the delayed plan is the zero plan shifted (spans, forks, branches, cache hits included), the zero plan equals `execute`'s, and `execute` reaches the same result with and without the delay; the sweep must hit spans, forks, branches, cache hits, gateway timeouts and db timeouts. |
| `states_are_checked_on_a_stale_entry_too`                       | A bad `states:` key or status on an endpoint no spec defines still fails compile; a valid one only produces the "stale" warning.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `schema_allows_null_pins`                                       | The committed JSON Schema accepts a scenario with `pins: null` and one with a pin list.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |

## `crates/kaanal-data/tests/api.rs` — OpenAPI parsing

| Test                                                              | What it proves                                                                                                                                                           |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `loads_endpoints_with_inlined_refs`                               | 4 endpoints load; lowest 2xx wins (404 ignored); `$ref`s fully inlined (no `$ref` key remains); array-item refs inlined too.                                             |
| `picks_201_and_no_body_cases`                                     | 201 chosen when it's the only 2xx; 204 has no schema.                                                                                                                    |
| `rejects_openapi_31`                                              | 3.1 fails, naming the version.                                                                                                                                           |
| `rejects_external_ref`                                            | `other.yaml#/User` fails ("external $ref").                                                                                                                              |
| `rejects_recursive_schema`                                        | Self-referencing schema fails fast ("recursive").                                                                                                                        |
| `rejects_missing_ref_target`                                      | Dangling ref fails ("not found").                                                                                                                                        |
| `rejects_branching_recursive_schema_fast`                         | A cycle through fan-out 2 fails immediately, not after 2^depth work.                                                                                                     |
| `rejects_ref_expansion_bomb`                                      | An _acyclic_ chain doubling each level (2³⁰ nodes — "billion laughs") hits the 10 000-expansion cap and errors instead of eating memory.                                 |
| `response_ref_chains_are_followed`                                | A response `$ref` to a response that is itself a `$ref` (`A → B`) reaches B's schema.                                                                                    |
| `response_ref_cycle_fails_instead_of_hanging`                     | `A → B → A` fails with "recursive $ref" instead of looping.                                                                                                              |
| `path_item_refs_are_followed_or_fail_loudly`                      | A path item `$ref: "#/paths/~1chain"` serves the same endpoint under `/alias`; an external path-item ref fails naming `/paths/~1alias` (never a silently dropped route). |
| `accepts_unquoted_numeric_version`                                | `openapi: 3.0` (YAML number, not string) is accepted.                                                                                                                    |
| `rejects_path_without_leading_slash`                              | `users:` (no `/`) fails with a clear message.                                                                                                                            |
| `response_example_beats_schema_examples`                          | A media-type `example` or `examples` replaces the schema's own `example`/`examples`, so the generator serves it first (D1.8).                                            |
| `a_relative_server_url_prefixes_every_path`                       | `servers: [{ url: /api/v3 }]` turns `/pets` into `/api/v3/pets` (R1.61).                                                                                                 |
| `an_absolute_server_url_contributes_its_path_only`                | `https://api.example.com:8443/v1/` adds `/v1`, not the host or the trailing slash.                                                                                       |
| `no_servers_a_root_url_or_a_host_only_url_add_no_prefix`          | No `servers`, an empty list, `/`, a host-only URL and a server with no `url` all leave the path alone.                                                                   |
| `only_the_first_server_counts`                                    | With two servers, the first one's path is used.                                                                                                                          |
| `server_variables_use_their_defaults`                             | `https://{env}.example.com/{ver}` with defaults `api` and `v2` gives `/v2`.                                                                                              |
| `a_variable_without_a_default_is_an_error`                        | `url: "/{ver}"` with no `variables` fails, naming `ver` and "default".                                                                                                   |
| `a_prefix_that_is_still_a_template_is_an_error`                   | A default that is itself `{x}` is refused.                                                                                                                               |
| `a_dangling_ref_in_media_examples_fails_the_load_with_a_position` | `examples: { one: { $ref: ... } }` naming a missing component fails the load at `/paths/~1a/get` with "not found".                                                       |
| `query_params_come_from_operation_and_path_item`                  | Query parameters of the operation and of its path item are both read.                                                                                                    |
| `query_params_follow_component_refs`                              | A parameter that is a `$ref` to a component is followed.                                                                                                                 |
| `non_query_and_non_integer_params_are_skipped`                    | Header/path params and object-typed query params are skipped; a string is kept with `numeric` false.                                                                     |
| `schema_ref_params_are_resolved`                                  | A query parameter whose `schema` is a `$ref` is resolved before its type is read.                                                                                        |
| `integer_bounds_are_read`                                         | `minimum`, `maximum` and `default` of a parameter are read.                                                                                                              |
| `non_query_params_with_unresolvable_schema_refs_still_load`       | A header/path/cookie parameter with an external or dangling schema `$ref` does not fail the load (review regression, fixed in 1cb501f).                                  |
| `query_param_with_dangling_schema_ref_is_skipped`                 | A query parameter with a dangling schema `$ref` is skipped, not an error.                                                                                                |
| `unresolvable_parameter_entries_are_skipped_not_fatal`            | A parameter entry that is an external or dangling `$ref`, on a `POST` and a `GET`, is skipped: the spec loads and the `GET` is still paged by its other parameters.      |
| `date_after_param_is_not_a_cursor`                                | A string `after` of format date, date-time or time is skipped; a plain string `after` is still a cursor.                                                                 |

## `crates/kaanal-data/tests/generate/` — data generation

One test target (`cargo test -p kaanal-data --test generate`), one module per topic in `tests/generate/` (R3.8):

| Module       | What it covers                                                                          |
| ------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `common.rs`  | The rich schema.                                                                        |
| `basics.rs`  | Seeds, validation, allOf, write-only.                                                   |
| `fields.rs`  | Formats, examples, heuristics, urls, params, odd schemas.                               |
| `numbers.rs` | Floats, integers, counts, bounds, lat/lng, lengths.                                     |
| `people.rs`  | Cities, addresses, emails, names, `fit_length`.                                         |
| `pattern.rs` | `pattern`: matches over 1000 seeds, kept email, pinned values, length bounds, warnings. |
| `formats.rs` | Examples by seed, newer formats, nullable, additionalProperties.                        |
| `one_of.rs`  | `oneOf`, `anyOf` and discriminators.                                                    |

| Test                                                                      | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `same_seed_same_value_different_seed_differs`                             | Seed reproducibility both ways.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `output_validates_against_schema`                                         | 200 seeds of a rich schema all pass `jsonschema` validation (rule R7.2: invalid output is a bug).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `allof_merges_properties`                                                 | `allOf` parts merge into one object with both property sets.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `allof_merges_non_object_parts`                                           | R6.10a: `allOf: [{type: string}, {maxLength: 5}]` validates (≤ 5 chars); an enum part plus a description part gives an enum value.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `allof_parts_describe_one_person_and_keep_siblings`                       | `firstName` and `lastName`/`email` in different (nested) parts describe one person; the `id` property and `required` next to `allOf` are kept; 100 seeds validate.                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `write_only_properties_are_left_out_of_responses`                         | R6.10b: a `writeOnly: true` property is never generated (it is not in `required`, so bodies validate); `writeOnly: false` is.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `formats_look_right`                                                      | date-time is 20 chars `…T…Z`; uuid is 36 chars with the v4 nibble.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `example_and_enum_win`                                                    | `example` is returned verbatim; single-choice `enum` is honoured.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `field_name_heuristics`                                                   | `email`/`user_email` → `*@example.com`; `firstName` alphabetic; `pincode` 6 digits; `createdAt` in 2025–2026; `price` 99–4999; `phone` `+91 …`; `currency` INR; city non-empty.                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `url_fields_have_no_empty_path_segment`                                   | A bare `url` field gives `https://example.com/N` (no `//`); `avatarUrl` gives `…/avatar/N`; `urlurl` strips only one suffix (`…/url/N`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `apply_params_sets_matching_fields`                                       | Path params overwrite same-named fields with type conversion (`"42"` → `42`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `apply_params_keeps_integer_when_param_is_not_numeric`                    | `/users/abc` never corrupts a numeric `id`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `deep_or_odd_schemas_do_not_panic`                                        | Empty schema, `minItems=maxItems=0`, and `minimum > maximum` all return something sane.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `huge_max_items_stays_small`                                              | `maxItems: 1_000_000` still yields ≤ 20 items (the cap).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `only_real_id_fields_get_ids`                                             | `userId`/`orderId` get id treatment; `valid`/`paid` do not (whole-word suffix matching of `*id`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `minimum_above_default_range_still_varies`                                | A `minimum: 5000` above the default range still produces _varying_ values ≥ 5000.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `adding_properties_keeps_other_values`                                    | The per-field seed rule (R4.4): shared fields keep identical values when properties are added. Reordering is unobservable today (`properties` is a `BTreeMap`, no `preserve_order`), so it is not tested.                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `exclusive_bounds_are_strict`                                             | OpenAPI 3.0 boolean `exclusiveMinimum/Maximum`: int range collapses to exactly 2; floats stay strictly inside.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `whole_number_float_and_fractional_integer_bounds_are_honoured`           | Integer bounds (R6.10c): `0.0`/`100.0` count (validated by `jsonschema`), `1.5..3.5` gives 2–3, exclusive `2.0..4.0` gives exactly 3, `maximum: u64::MAX` validates, `minimum: u64::MAX` saturates to `i64::MAX`; a lone `maximum: -5` moves the range (varied values, all ≤ -5) instead of pinning to -5.                                                                  |
| `counts_accept_whole_number_floats_and_max_items_zero_is_empty`           | `maxItems: 0` (and `0.0`) gives `[]`; float `minItems`/`maxItems` and `minLength`/`maxLength` are honoured.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `number_bounds_are_honoured_at_the_edges`                                 | `price` with only `maximum: 50` validates and still varies; `-1e308..1e308` does not panic and validates; an exclusive `0..0.015` gives the midpoint `0.0075`.                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `lat_lng_get_bounds_and_unbounded_output_is_unchanged`                    | Unbounded `lat`/`longitude` with the default `en-IN` pack equal the old fixed-range draw bit for bit (its `geo` block keeps India's bounds); bounded lat/lng validate against `minimum`/`maximum` and keep 6 decimals.                                                                                                                                                      |
| `min_length_pads_in_one_pass_and_is_capped`                               | `minLength: 40` + `maxLength: 50` validates; `minLength: 1e9` returns exactly 10 000 characters in well under a second (R6.10d).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `length_limits_never_break_formats`                                       | `maxLength: 8` doesn't truncate an email; `minLength: 50` doesn't pad a uuid; name-heuristic emails are also protected (rule R4.5).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `city_state_pincode_and_address_describe_the_same_place`                  | 2000 seeds: one object always has a consistent (city, state, pincode) triple, the address ends in that city and contains the object's `street`; cities still vary across seeds.                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `names_and_email_describe_the_same_person`                                | 300 seeds: `name` is `firstName lastName` and `email` starts with `first.last`; people still vary across seeds.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `unknown_formats_still_get_length_limits`                                 | `format: password` + `minLength`/`maxLength` stays within the lengths (only built formats skip them).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `huge_or_deeply_nested_arrays_are_bounded`                                | `minItems: 1e9` yields at most 10 000 items; five nested 20-item levels stay under 1 MB (per-body value budget).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `email_of_two_non_latin_names_is_one_user_n`                              | A pack whose names are all CJK with no `latin:` gives `user<n>@example.com`, one `user<n>`, never `user7.user77`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `fit_length_counts_characters_not_bytes`                                  | `maxLength: 4` on a CJK, emoji or combining-mark name cuts at 4 characters (never mid-character); `minLength: 6` pads to 6 characters.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `examples_pick_one_entry_by_seed_and_are_stable`                          | `examples` as an array or an OpenAPI `{ value }` map gives at least two different entries over 60 seeds, the same one per seed; `example` beats `examples`, which beats `enum`.                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `each_new_format_validates_against_the_schema`                            | `hostname`, `ipv4`, `ipv6`, `byte`, `password` and `duration` pass `jsonschema` with format checking over 50 seeds; `byte` is padded base64 and `duration` starts with `P`.                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `ipv4_is_in_a_documentation_range`                                        | 100 seeds all start `198.51.100.` or `203.0.113.`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `nullable_emits_a_real_value`                                             | `nullable: true`, `type: [integer, null]` and `[null, boolean]` always give a value of the real type.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
