| `topology_reports_expected_visits_per_endpoint`                | `/topology` lists `endpoints[].visits`: with `calls: 3, prob: 0.5` on app to db, `GET /users/{id}` shows nginx 1, app 1, db 1.5; HEAD copies are not listed.                                                 |
| `an_edge_entry_is_set_listed_and_cleared`                      | (`chaos_links.rs`) PUT with `edge`, jitter and a cap, listed under `edges` (live and effective), cleared with `edge_from`/`edge_to`.                                                                         |
| `a_cap_takes_a_unit_like_the_yaml_and_is_rejected_like_it`     | (`chaos_links.rs`, F7) A string cap (`"500KB/s"`) is stored as 512000 bytes; `"2MB/s"` over the edge is a 400, `"0KB"` a 400, `"5XB"` and `-5` a 422 naming `bandwidth_cap`.                                 |
| `a_link_chaos_hop_is_flagged_in_the_trace`                     | Latency 30 ms and jitter 2 ms on the only edge into `db`: its net hop says `chaos +30ms` and `link jitter ±2ms`, the entry has `DI`, and the other edge's hop has no note.                                   |
| `a_link_cap_and_a_partition_are_flagged`                       | A cap below the edge's bandwidth shows on the link hop (`link capped at 500000 B/s`); a partition is a 504 with `FI`.                                                                                        |
| `a_downed_replica_is_skipped_listed_and_cleared`               | (`chaos_replicas.rs`) A keyed PUT with `replica` downs one copy: requests still succeed, `GET /chaos` lists it under `replicas`, all three down gives 503, `DELETE ?node&replica` brings it back.            |
| `bad_replica_requests_are_400`                                 | A replica without a node, out of range, or with an endpoint is a 400 with the reason; so is a clear with a bad replica.                                                                                      |
| `the_trace_and_the_log_name_the_replica`                       | (`chaos_replicas.rs`) With 3 db replicas the trace names `db#N` for several N and never `app#`; the logged hops carry `replica` on db only.                                                                  |
| `chaos_latency_on_a_replica_is_attributed_to_its_hop`          | Latency set on every db replica shows as `chaos +40ms` on the db hop (the attribution reads the replica entry).                                                                                              |
| `the_topology_reports_replicas_only_for_replicated_nodes`      | `/topology` gives db `replicas: 3` and app no `replicas` key.                                                                                                                                                |
| `events_stream_reports_requests`                               | SSE: a request to `/users/7` appears as `event: request` with the ordered node list `["nginx","app","db"]`, measured `ms` ≥ 5 and `endpoint` `GET /users/{id}` (the route template, not the URL).            |
| `events_without_token_is_401`                                  | SSE needs the token too.                                                                                                                                                                                     |

_Embedded UI page:_

| Test                                                                        | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `ui_page_cannot_be_framed_and_sends_no_referrer`                            | CSP `frame-ancestors 'none'`; `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`.                                                                                                                                        |
| `ui_page_is_served_without_token`                                           | 200 HTML containing the app name, `EventSource`, and fragment-token reading.                                                                                                                         |
| `ui_page_avoids_unsafe_dom_apis_and_external_assets`                        | Static guard: no `innerHTML`/`eval(`/inline handlers/`localStorage`/any `http(s)://` URL in the page.                                                                                                |
| `ui_controls_have_accessible_names_and_dark_form_controls`                  | `color-scheme: light dark`, aria labels, `role="alert"`.                                                                                                                                             |
| `ui_keeps_stream_status_and_api_errors_on_separate_lines_and_uses_counters` | Structural guards on the page's JS: separate status/error elements, fixed-size counter buckets, node names as `Map` keys.                                                                            |
| `config_seed_changes_generated_bodies`                                      | Same `seed:` → same `/users/42` body; a different `seed:` → different data (path `id` still 42).                                                                                                     |
| `gatewayless_requests_enter_at_the_service`                                 | No gateway: `GET /users/42` returns 200 and the trace starts at `app.proc=` and includes `db.read=` (no gateway hop).                                                                                |
| `a_lone_monolith_serves_its_spec`                                           | A topology of one `monolith` with a spec and no gateway serves its routes over HTTP and traces its own `proc`, then the inline op (one comma in the trace).                                          |
| `trace_is_capped_and_counts_what_it_left_out`                               | A 2 003-hop chain returns a trace of exactly 4 087 bytes ending `,...+1664 hops` (hand-counted cap, N exact, D13).                                                                                   |
| `trace_brackets_parallel_branches_and_marks_cache_hits`                     | `parallel` prints `[a,b\|c,d]`, a cache hit prints `c.hit=`; with `pay` down the cancelled sibling shows as `[\|net=0.0ms]` and the trace ends `pay.error=503`.                                      |
| `trace_drops_blocks_a_failure_cancelled_before_they_started`                | A nested `parallel` that a sibling's failure cancelled before any of its hops started is left out of the trace (no `[                                                                                |
| `error_map_shapes_status_and_body`                                          | `error_map` by node (status and body), by class (`5xx`), none (status passes through); the trace keeps the real failure.                                                                             |
| `head_inherits_the_get_entry`                                               | A HEAD with no entry copies GET's `override`; an explicit `HEAD` entry keeps GET's owner and op (R6.8).                                                                                              |
| `endpoints_file_and_inline_serve_the_same_chain`                            | The same entry inline and in `kaanal.endpoints.yaml` gives the same trace.                                                                                                                           |
| `realistic_runs_replay_under_controlled_with_the_printed_seed`              | `determinism: realistic` draws a seed per start (`drawn_seed`), bodies keep `seed:`, and `controlled` with the drawn seed repeats the 16 request statuses.                                           |
| `gatewayless_custom_chain_runs_in_order`                                    | Without a gateway a custom chain runs in order after the owner's `proc`; an `override` still runs the chain.                                                                                         |
| `serve_without_a_signing_key_fails_closed`                                  | `serve` with an auth node and `auth_key: None` fails with "auth node 'login' needs a signing key (kaanal up provides one)" instead of serving unsigned logins (X5).                                  |
| `login_with_json_returns_a_token_that_verifies_against_the_served_jwks`     | `POST /login` with JSON (extra `grant_type` ignored) gives 200, the chain trace, `expires_in` 7200, `scope`, and a token that `jsonwebtoken` verifies against the served JWKS with the right claims. |
| `login_with_form_data_works_and_omits_scope_without_scopes`                 | Form bodies work (case-insensitive type, percent escapes decoded); a user without scopes gets no `scope` in body or token; node claims apply.                                                        |
| `wrong_or_unknown_credentials_are_401_after_the_chain_latency`              | A wrong password and an unknown user both give 401 `invalid_credentials` after at least the 50 ms chain latency.                                                                                     |
| `missing_or_text_plain_content_type_is_415`                                 | No `Content-Type`, `text/plain` and `application/jsonx` give 415 after the chain latency.                                                                                                            |
| `malformed_duplicate_or_non_string_fields_are_400_without_echo`             | Duplicate, non-string, missing, truncated and array bodies (JSON and form) give exactly `{"error":"invalid_request"}`; the password is never echoed.                                                 |
| `oversized_login_body_is_413`                                               | A body of exactly 16 KiB logs in; one byte more is 413 `request_too_large`.                                                                                                                          |
| `every_login_response_is_no_store`                                          | 200, 401, 400, 413 and 415 all carry `Cache-Control: no-store` and `Pragma: no-cache`; the JWKS does not.                                                                                            |
| `chain_failure_beats_body_errors_and_goes_through_error_map`                | With the auth node down, a good, oversized, header-less or broken body all give the chain's 503; an `error_map` on `POST /login` reshapes the failure.                                               |
| `auth_latency_chaos_slows_login_and_jwks`                                   | 200 ms latency chaos on the auth node makes login and JWKS take at least 250 ms.                                                                                                                     |
| `override_replaces_the_login_responder`                                     | An `override` on `POST /login` answers without reading the body (no `Content-Type` needed) and is still `no-store`.                                                                                  |
| `login_and_jwks_send_cors_and_answer_preflight`                             | A login preflight is 204 and login and JWKS responses send `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`.                                                                                                         |
| `passwords_never_reach_trace_events_or_control_api`                         | After a good and a wrong login, no password appears in a trace header, the event stream, `/topology` or `/chaos`.                                                                                    |
| `jwks_head_trace_and_route_listing`                                         | The JWKS GET has the chain trace and one RS256 key; HEAD is 200 with no body; `/topology` lists both built-in routes.                                                                                |
| `an_auth_only_config_serves_login`                                          | A config of one auth node (no gateway) serves a login traced as `login.proc=` and its token verifies.                                                                                                |
| `get_on_login_is_405`                                                       | `GET /login` is 405.                                                                                                                                                                                 |

_Capacity (live engine):_

| Test                                                            | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| --------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `a_full_pool_queues_then_overflows`                             | Three simultaneous requests to a `pool: { size: 1, queue: 1 }` database give two 200s and one 503; the queued one takes at least two hops and traces `db.wait=`, the first has no wait.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `zero_load_trace_has_no_wait_pieces`                            | A single request to a roomy pool has no `wait` anywhere in its trace.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `rate_limit_429_carries_retry_after_and_error_map_can_drop_it`  | The second request through `rate_limit: { rps: 1, burst: 1 }` is 429 with `Retry-After: 1` and ends `nginx.error=429`; an `error_map` turning 429 into 503 drops the header.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `a_chaos_down_node_beats_the_rate_limit`                        | A chaos-down node answers 503 every time: a chaos failure takes no token, so it never becomes 429.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `a_dropped_client_does_not_leak_a_slot`                         | Clients that give up after 30 ms leave their hops running; once they end, the slot is free and the next request has no `wait`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `many_parallel_requests_all_complete_through_one_slot`          | 40 simultaneous requests through a `concurrency: 1` service all answer 200 (unbounded queue, no overflow).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `failing_branch_answers_before_the_running_sibling_ends`        | In a `parallel` block, a failing branch answers at its failure (~60 ms) while a 400 ms sibling is still running (bound: under 350 ms).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `cors_exposes_retry_after_to_browsers`                          | A 429 from a rate limit lists `retry-after` (and `x-kaanal-trace`) in `Access-Control-Expose-Headers`, so browser code can read it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `parallel_failure_tie_goes_to_the_first_branch_live`            | Live: two branches fail at t=0 (zero costs), the first through a zero-duration hop; the answer is the first branch's 503 and the trace ends `s.error=503`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `load_pooled_node_keeps_latency_and_trace_accurate` _(ignored)_ | 3000 requests/s open-loop for 4 s (12 000 requests), every other one carrying an empty `X-Kaanal-Scenario` (so the scoped path is under load too), through a `concurrency: 8` db of 2 ms ops (75% busy): all answer 200, every trace keeps `db.read=2.0ms`, no trace claims more time than the client waited, and the real latency stays within 25 ms (median) and 150 ms (p99) of the trace; prints the lag and wait percentiles. Run: `cargo test -p kaanal-server --release --test http -- --ignored --nocapture load_`. A third of the requests (every `i % 3 == 2`) go to `GET /users`, pinned `empty` by a scenario the request names in `X-Kaanal-Scenario`; each of those must answer 200 with a trace ending `pinned=empty` (and a db scan hop of exactly 2 ms), the rest must carry no `pinned=`, exactly a third must hit the pin, and the same zero-error and lag bounds (p50 25 ms, p99 150 ms) hold. Two runs: 12 000 requests, no errors, p50 10.2-10.4 ms, p99 16.7-18.2 ms over the trace. |

_Endpoint keys (B2b-1):_

| Test                                           | What it proves                                                                                                                                           |
| ---------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `a_malformed_endpoint_is_400_on_set_and_clear` | `PUT /chaos` with endpoint `GET` and `DELETE /chaos?endpoint=nope` are 400 (the shared strict `endpoint_key`); they used to be a 404 and a silent no-op. |

## `crates/kaanal-server/tests/scenarios_load.rs` — scenario loading

Through `kaanal_server::check` on temporary project directories.

| Test                                         | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| -------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `absent_dir_is_fine`                         | No `scenarios/` directory: no scenarios, no error.                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `discovery_takes_only_plain_yaml_files`      | Only `ok.yaml` and `bom.yaml` load (the BOM is tolerated; its orphan `error_kind` is the one warning, naming `bom` and `error_rate`); the invalid files in a subdirectory, `.hidden.yaml`, `x.yaml~`, `.#lock.yaml` and `notes.txt` are ignored. |
| `invalid_stem_names_the_file`                | A stem that is not a valid name is a load error placed in that file, not a skip.                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `oversized_file_is_refused`                  | A file over 1 MiB is refused, naming it.                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `too_many_files_are_refused`                 | More than 128 files is a load error.                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `inline_and_file_with_one_name_clash`        | A scenario `pd` both inline and as `scenarios/pd.yaml` is a load error: "both the config and a scenario file".                                                                                                                                   |
| `file_errors_point_into_the_file`            | An unknown node `ghost` in the fourth line of a file is an error "unknown node 'ghost'" attributed to that file and placed at line 4, column 13.                                                                                                 |
| `file_parse_errors_carry_their_position`     | A YAML error (unknown key `bogus` on line 2) in a scenario file is attributed to that file with line 2.                                                                                                                                          |
| `inline_errors_point_into_kaanal_yaml`       | An `error_rate: 2` in an inline scenario is an error naming `error_rate` with no file, and `in_source` places it on the last line of `kaanal.yaml` (the value's line).                                                                           |
| `inline_endpoint_checks_and_warnings`        | An inline entry for `get /users/{id}` (lowercase method) with only `error_kind: hang` loads with one warning naming scenario `pd`; an entry for `GET /nope` is an error "unknown endpoint".                                                      |
| `bad_inline_name_is_rejected`                | An inline scenario named `Active` is a load error "invalid scenario name".                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `a_symlinked_scenarios_dir_is_followed`      | When `scenarios` is a symlink to a directory holding `x.yaml`, the file loads (its orphan `error_kind` warning names `x`); returns early where symlinks cannot be created.                                                                       |
| `symlinked_files_are_skipped_with_a_warning` | A symlinked `link.yaml` next to a good `ok.yaml` is skipped with the one warning "skipped symlink scenarios/link.yaml"; `ok.yaml` still loads.                                                                                                   |
| `near_miss_extensions_warn`                  | `a.yml`, `B.YAML` and `c.Yaml` are each skipped with a "must end in .yaml" warning (three warnings, no error).                                                                                                                                   |
| `non_utf8_names_warn`                        | (Unix only) A non-UTF-8 file name is skipped with a warning.                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `non_utf8_content_is_an_error_in_the_file`   | A file whose content is not UTF-8 is a load error in that file.                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `the_size_limit_is_exactly_one_mib`          | Exactly 1 MiB loads, 1 MiB + 1 byte is refused.                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

## `crates/kaanal-server/tests/scenarios_http/` — scenario control API

One test target (`cargo test -p kaanal-server --test scenarios_http`), one module per topic in `tests/scenarios_http/` (R3.8):

| Module        | What it covers                                                                             |
| ------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `common.rs`   | Shared helpers: a scenario server in a scratch folder.                                     |
| `stacking.rs` | Activation stacks over live chaos, the snapshot rules, races, reset.                       |
| `save.rs`     | `scenarios/save`: layers, overwrite refusal, `from` sources, leased-field warnings.        |
| `api.rs`      | The active stack, runtime scenarios, events, chaos responses, reset and rate-limit refill. |
| `ttl.rs`      | Activation and chaos `ttl` leases.                                                         |

End to end over HTTP with a live server on temporary directories.

| Test                                                                             | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `activate_stacks_and_deactivate_restores`                                        | Two scenarios, `slow-db` and `db-down`, are defined; activating `db-down` over HTTP answers 200 with `active: ["db-down"]` and `effective` showing db `down`, and requests now get 503. Deactivating answers 204 and requests are 200 again. An unknown name is 404 on activate and an inactive one is a 204 on deactivate.                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `live_override_calms_a_scenario`                                                 | With `db-down` active, a live `down: false` alone leaves requests at 503; adding `mode: override` makes them 200.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `mode_is_sticky_until_merge_is_said`                                             | A stored `mode: override` survives a later PUT with no `mode`; `mode: merge` clears it from the live view; `mode: replace` is 400.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `delete_chaos_leaves_scenarios_and_delete_scenarios_leaves_live`                 | `DELETE /chaos` (204) empties the live layer but the active stack and the 503 stay; `DELETE /scenarios` (204) empties the stack but the live `latency_ms: 100` stays and requests return to 200.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `concurrent_chaos_puts_all_land`                                                 | 16 concurrent `PUT /chaos` (multi-thread runtime) hit 8 keys (3 nodes, 3 endpoints, 2 node-at-endpoint pairs), two fields each (`latency_ms`, `error_rate`): every key holds both fields afterwards (no lost update, S11) and `effective` agrees (nginx 12 ms).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `put_scenario_racing_activate_ends_consistent`                                   | 20 or 21 `PUT /scenarios/x` (alternating a `down` and a 100 ms definition) race 20 `activate` calls over two rounds; every call is 200 and afterwards `active` is `["x"]` and `effective` equals exactly the last PUT's definition (down in one round, 100 ms in the other).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `read_your_writes`                                                               | 50 times: `PUT /chaos` db `down` true/false alternately, then a request right after; it returns 503 or 200 to match the write that just returned (S10).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `a_request_keeps_its_snapshot_across_a_swap`                                     | A slow in-flight request keeps its plan when a scenario is activated mid-flight; the next request sees the new state.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `reset_clears_everything_and_repeats_the_seeded_pattern`                         | A 50% error rate on db gives a mix of 200 and 503 over 20 requests. After activating a scenario, `POST /reset` answers 204, the active stack and the live layer are empty, and the same 20-request pattern repeats exactly (the seeded request counter restarted).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `scenario_names_are_checked_everywhere`                                          | `PUT /scenarios/{bad}` for `..%2Fx`, `CON`, `Payments`, `a%2Fb`, `save`, `active` answers 400, 405 or 422 (never defines it); `save` with a bad `name` (`../x`, `a/b`, `Con`, `save`, `reset`, empty, 65 characters) is 400; the scenario list stays `[]` and nothing is written under `scenarios/`.                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `save_writes_sparse_layers_and_refuses_overwrite`                                | `save` of live chaos writes `mine.yaml` with only the set field (`latency_ms`, no `error_*` or `down`), answers 200 with `name` and `path`, and registers it as a `file` scenario. A second save is 409; `force` replaces the content and leaves no temp file; an inline name is 400 even with `force`. `from: effective` over two active scenarios and a calming live override omits the calmed key, and loading that file in a fresh server gives the same `effective`.                                     |
| `a_saved_replica_entry_round_trips`                                              | (`save.rs`) Live chaos on replica 1 saves as an entry with `node` and `replica`, and a fresh server loads and activates that file.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `save_from_effective_keeps_an_edge_jitter_and_writes_no_status`                  | `save` from `effective` writes an edge's jitter and no `error_status`, and the file loads.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `scenario_save_round_trips_an_edge_entry`                                        | A live edge entry saves as `edge: { from, to }` with `jitter_ms`, and the saved file loads and resolves to the same effective edge.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `save_from_effective_refuses_banded_keys_and_from_live_refuses_override`         | `from: effective` of two stacked faults on db (different kinds) is 400 naming `db`; plain `save` (`from: live`) while a live override exists is 400. `from: {scenario: p}` copies a definition (200, writes `p2.yaml`), an unknown scenario is 404, and only `p2.yaml` exists afterwards.                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `save_from_effective_skips_a_pair_equal_to_its_node_and_refuses_a_different_one` | With db `down` live and the same `down` on the db@endpoint pair, `from: effective` writes the node entry only (no `endpoint` in the file); once the pair adds 50 ms it differs from its node and `save` answers 400 with "compiled together".                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `save_with_clear_live_does_not_double_count`                                     | `save` with `clear_live: true` answers 200 and empties the live layer; activating the saved scenario then shows db `latency_ms` 100, not 200.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `put_scenarios_active_sets_the_stack_atomically`                                 | `PUT /scenarios/active ["a","b"]` sets the stack (200); `["a","ghost"]` is 404 and the stack stays `["a","b"]`; `[]` empties it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `static_active_routes_win_over_the_name_capture`                                 | `GET /scenarios/active` returns the stack view (an `active` key) and `PUT /scenarios/active` sets the stack, rather than treating `active` as a scenario name; `DELETE /scenarios/active` is 405.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `endpoint_latency_from_two_scenarios_clamps`                                     | Two active scenarios each adding 240 s to `GET /users/{id}`: `effective` shows the endpoint at 300 000 ms (clamped to 5 min).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `scenario_events_are_emitted`                                                    | With an SSE stream open, activate, deactivate and reset produce `event: scenario` messages carrying `"action":"activate"`, `"deactivate"` and `"reset"`, and the scenario name `a`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `runtime_scenarios_define_list_show_and_undefine`                                | `PUT /scenarios/rt` defines a runtime scenario (200, one warning for an `error_kind` without a rate). The list is exactly `inl` (source `inline`) then `rt` (source `runtime`, with description, entry count and an empty `pins: []`); `GET` shows its entries sorted by node id. An unknown node is 404, an out-of-range rate and an empty `chaos` are 400, and redefining or deleting the inline name is 409. Deleting an unknown name is 404; deleting the active `rt` is 204 and drops it from the stack. |
| `chaos_responses_carry_effective_and_warnings`                                   | With a 30 ms scenario active, `PUT /chaos` adding 20 ms returns `effective` db 50 ms and no warnings; an orphan `error_kind` on `app` gives one warning containing `node 'app'`; a later PUT on `nginx` reports none (only the key just written); `GET /chaos` also carries `effective` (50 ms).                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `reset_undefines_runtime_scenarios_but_keeps_inline_and_file_ones`               | With a runtime, an inline and a file scenario, two of them active: `reset` is 204, the list is `["inl","fromfile"]`, the stack is empty and the runtime one is 404.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `save_from_is_tagged`                                                            | A scenario may be named `live`: `from: {scenario: "live"}` copies it (200). A bare name `"someone"`, `{"live":null}`, `{"effective":null}`, an extra key, a non-string name, `{}` and the string `"scenario"` are all 422 with "from must be"; only `copy.yaml` was written.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `force_save_does_not_follow_a_planted_tmp_symlink`                               | With a symlink planted at the temp path pointing at a victim file, a forced save still answers 200 with the new content, the victim keeps its text, and no extra file is left (skips where symlinks cannot be created).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `reset_refills_the_rate_limit_bucket`                                            | A `rate_limit: { rps: 1, burst: 1 }` gateway answers 200 then 429; after `reset` (204) the next request is 200 and the one after is 429 again.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `reset_during_in_flight_requests_does_not_hang`                                  | `reset` while six requests are in flight or queued (burst 6, `concurrency: 1`, queue 10, 300 ms latency): `reset` is 204, all six finish with 200 within 10 s, and after a second reset a new request is 200.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `put_over_an_active_scenario_emits_an_update_event`                              | Redefining an active scenario (`PUT` 1 ms then 2 ms) emits a `"action":"update"` event and `effective` shows the new 2 ms.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `mode_only_patches`                                                              | A bare `{mode: merge}` on a key with no live entry answers 200 and stores nothing; a bare `{mode: override}` on a key a scenario takes down turns requests from 503 to 200.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `ttl_expires_and_chaos_clears`                                                   | With an SSE stream open, `activate` with `{"ttl":"300ms"}` answers 200 with `expires_in_ms` for the scenario between 1 and 300; the request is 503 at first, then 200 once it expires; `active` and `expires_in_ms` are empty and the stream carries an `"action":"expire"` event.                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `reactivate_restarts_or_clears_the_lease`                                        | A 300 ms lease re-activated at 150 ms with 600 ms is still active 300 ms later and gone soon after; a lease followed by a plain `activate` (empty body) leaves `expires_in_ms` empty and the scenario still active after a second.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `ttl_bounds_and_shape`                                                           | `99ms`, `25h`, `abc`, a number, an unknown key and invalid JSON are each 400 and leave nothing active; `100ms`, `24h`, `{}` and `{"ttl":null}` are 200.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `lease_dropped_with_the_activation`                                              | A 1 h lease is gone from `expires_in_ms` after `deactivate`, `PUT scenarios/active`, `DELETE scenarios` and `reset`; a runtime scenario with a 300 ms lease that is deleted before it fires leaves nothing active and requests at 200 half a second later.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `lease_and_live_write_both_survive`                                              | A 300 ms lease on `slow` and a live `down` write on db: after the lease expires the stack is empty, the live `down: true` is still stored and requests are 503.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `list_shows_expires_in_ms`                                                       | With `db-down` leased for 1 h, `GET /scenarios` gives it `expires_in_ms` between 3 599 000 and 3 600 000 and gives the unleased `slow` no such key.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `put_with_ttl_leases_only_the_units_it_sets`                                     | db `latency_ms: 200` set permanently (no `expires_in_ms` in the answer), then `{down: true, ttl: "30s"}`: the answer and `GET /chaos` carry `expires_in_ms` with only `down` (29 000-30 000 ms), and the latency stays.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `plain_put_makes_the_unit_permanent`                                             | A timed `down` followed by a plain `{down: true}`: no `expires_in_ms` in the answer or the live view, and db stays down (Review Focus 1).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `reput_with_a_new_ttl_replaces_the_deadline`                                     | `latency` leased for 30 s, then 1 h (3 599 000-3 600 000 ms left), then 1 s (at most 1 000 ms): each PUT replaces the deadline.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `bad_ttl_is_400_and_changes_nothing`                                             | `0ms`, `25h` and `abc` on both PUT routes are 400 with an error naming `ttl`, and the live view is unchanged.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `keyed_put_with_ttl_leases_its_key`                                              | `PUT /chaos` on db@`GET /users/{id}` with `error_rate`, `mode: override` and `ttl: 1m` leases `error` and `mode` on that pair; the node's own entry is another key and is not leased.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `delete_node_and_reset_drop_leases`                                              | For each of `DELETE /chaos/db`, `DELETE /chaos?node=db`, `DELETE /chaos` and `POST /reset`: a leased `down` is removed with its entry, and a later `latency_ms` PUT on db (another unit, so only `commit`'s prune can drop the old lease) shows no `expires_in_ms`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `save_with_clear_live_drops_leases`                                              | A leased `down`, then `save` with `clear_live`, then a `latency_ms` PUT on db: no `expires_in_ms`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `put_down_true_then_false_removes_the_entry`                                     | `down: true` then `down: false` on db leaves `GET /chaos` with no `nodes.db`; the keyed form (node@endpoint) does the same and leaves `node_at` empty (L7: a live `down: false` is unset).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `down_false_keeps_other_fields`                                                  | `latency_ms: 200` with `down: true`, then `down: false`: the entry stays with latency 200 and `down` false.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `down_false_does_not_undo_a_scenarios_down`                                      | A scenario holds db down; a live `down: true` then `down: false` leaves the live layer empty, `effective` still says db is down and a request through it is 503.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `down_false_with_a_ttl_leaves_no_down_lease`                                     | `latency` and `down` leased for 1 h, then `{down: false, ttl: "30s"}`: the answer and `GET /chaos` keep the `latency` lease and show no `down` lease (prune drops the lease of the unset field).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `down_false_removes_the_down_lease`                                              | `latency` and `down` leased together, then `{down: false}`: `latency` keeps its lease and `down` has none. An entry with only a leased `down` emptied by `{down: false, ttl: "30s"}` is removed, and a later `latency_ms` PUT on the key shows no `expires_in_ms`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `save_from_live_warns_that_leased_fields_become_permanent`                       | `save` of unleased live chaos answers `warnings: []`; with `down`, `error_rate`, `error_kind` and `mode: merge` leased it answers exactly `3 fields have a ttl; the file has none` (fields, not units; `mode` not counted), and the file holds `down` with no `ttl`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `save_from_effective_warns_only_when_it_includes_leased_chaos`                   | `save --from effective` answers `warnings: []` with no lease and `1 field has a ttl; the file has none` once a field is leased.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |

## `crates/kaanal-server/tests/scoping_http.rs` — scenario scoping

End to end over HTTP: scenarios `db-down` (503), `a` (503), `b` (504), `flaky`, defined in the config, on a temporary directory.

| Test                                                 | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ---------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `scoped_requests_get_their_own_chaos`                | `X-Kaanal-Scenario: db-down` is 503 and an empty header is 200 while nothing is active; after a global `activate db-down` the unscoped request is 503, the empty header still 200 and `a` still 503.                        |
| `scoped_ignores_the_live_layer`                      | With a live `error_rate: 1` on db, the unscoped request is 503 and the empty-header request is 200.                                                                                                                         |
| `stack_order_is_header_order`                        | `a,b` answers 504 and `b,a` answers 503 (the later name wins); `a,b,a` answers 503 and echoes `x-kaanal-scope: b,a`.                                                                                                        |
| `echo_header_and_cors`                               | A scoped response echoes `x-kaanal-scope: a`, lists it in `access-control-expose-headers` and an unscoped one has no such header; a preflight asking for `x-kaanal-scenario` is 204 with `access-control-allow-headers: *`. |
| `bad_scopes_are_400`                                 | `nope`, `Bad!`, `a,,b`, `a,`, 17 names, 257 bytes, a non-ASCII byte and two header lines are each 400 with CORS and `error: "bad scenario scope"`; the first names `unknown scenario 'nope'`.                               |
| `bad_scope_does_not_perturb_seeded_draws`            | A 50% live error rate gives the same 20 statuses after `reset` whether or not a bad-scope request (400) is sent before each one: a rejected header takes no counter draw.                                                   |
| `control_namespace_ignores_the_header`               | `GET /__kaanal/health` with a bad `x-kaanal-scenario` is still 200.                                                                                                                                                         |
| `definition_changes_reach_the_cache`                 | A runtime scenario `rt` is seen scoped as 503; after `PUT` of a calm definition it is 200, after `DELETE` it is 400, after redefining 503, after `reset` 400 again: the cache never serves an old definition.               |
| `force_save_reaches_the_cache`                       | Saving live chaos as `rt2` is seen scoped as 503; after changing the live chaos and a forced `save` over it, scoped `rt2` is 200.                                                                                           |
| `scoped_miss_racing_put_scenario_never_serves_stale` | Four tasks keep scoping `rt` (cache misses) while 30 alternating `PUT`s redefine it; right after each `PUT` the scoped answer matches that definition (503 or 200).                                                         |
| `scoped_equals_global_activation`                    | Twenty statuses from a seeded `flaky` activated globally equal twenty from `X-Kaanal-Scenario: flaky` after a `reset`: the scoped stack draws like the global one.                                                          |
| `parallel_workers_do_not_interfere`                  | Eight tasks, 25 requests each, alternate `db-down` (always 503) and an empty header (always 200) while the global `a` is activated and deactivated twenty times.                                                            |
| `more_than_the_cache_bound_still_answers_right`      | 17 scenarios give 272 two-name stacks (over the 256 bound, so the cache clears); every one answers right (503 when `s00` is in it, else 200).                                                                               |

## `crates/kaanal-server/tests/pins_http/` — pins over HTTP

One test target (`cargo test -p kaanal-server --test pins_http`), one module per topic in `tests/pins_http/` (R3.8):

| Module      | What it covers                                                                |
| ----------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `common.rs` | Shared helpers: a pins server and its config pieces.                          |
| `states.rs` | The pin states on a request and how pins meet chaos and wire failures.        |
| `load.rs`   | Config-load rules for pins, pins-only scenarios, scenario ttl expiry of pins. |
| `api.rs`    | The pins API and `cycle`.                                                     |
| `events.rs` | Saving pins, the events pins and cycles emit, no-op deletes.                  |

Pins on the data path and through the control API ([pins](files/kaanal-server/pins.md), [pin_api](files/kaanal-server/pin_api.md), [mock](files/kaanal-server/mock.md)). Scoped requests (`X-Kaanal-Scenario`) are used to pin through scenarios.

| Test                                                                       | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `loading_pin_answers_after_the_delay_without_a_timeout`                    | A scenario pin `loading` of 1.2 s under a gateway `timeout: 500ms`: the answer takes at least 1.15 s, is 200 with a real body, and the trace has no `timeout` or `error` and ends `pinned=loading`.                                                                                                                      |
| `slow_pin_adds_the_delay`                                                  | A `slow` pin of 300 ms: at least 280 ms, 200, trace ends `pinned=slow`; the same route unscoped stays fast (under 250 ms).                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `error_pin_defaults_and_overrides`                                         | Default `error` is 500 with `{"error":"pinned","state":"error"}` and no `Retry-After`; `states.error` gives 404 and its body; a 429 and a 503 pin carry `Retry-After: 1`.                                                                                                                                                |
| `empty_pin_from_schema_and_from_states_override`                           | `GET /users` pinned `empty` gives 200 `[]` with `pinned=empty`; `POST /orders` gives the route's normal 201 with the `states.empty.body`.                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `empty_pin_on_a_204_route_stays_204`                                       | A route overridden to 204 answers 204 with no body when pinned `empty`, trace `pinned=empty`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `empty_pin_on_static_override_route_derives_from_the_body`                 | A static route with `override: {ok, items:[1,2], total:5}` pinned `empty` gives `{ok: true, items: [], total: 0}`.                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `success_pin_hides_a_downed_node_and_a_429_limit_and_error_map`            | Without the pin a downed db is the `error_map` 502; scoped with `db-down,p-success` it is 200 with the real body while the trace still shows the error and ends `pinned=success`. On a gateway with a 1 rps limit, unpinned requests hit 429, pinned `success` ones are all 200.                                         |
| `pin_with_chaos_hang_on_the_endpoint_keeps_the_delay`                      | A front-door hang plus a 300 ms `loading` pin takes at least 750 ms (pin delay, then the 500 ms gateway timeout), and the answer is still the pinned 200.                                                                                                                                                                |
| `head_follows_the_get_pin`                                                 | A `HEAD` under a 503 pin on the `GET` has the same status, `Retry-After: 1`, `pinned=error` in the trace, and no body.                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `pin_does_not_change_seeded_draws`                                         | With `flaky` active and the counters reset, 20 plain statuses are recorded (a mix of 200 and failures); replaying with an extra `success` pin gives 200 every time while the trace shows an error exactly when the plain run failed; after deactivating the pin the next ten statuses equal the plain run's second half. |
| `trace_ends_with_pinned_and_event_carries_pin`                             | A pinned `error` request ends its trace `pinned=error`, and the SSE request event has `"pin":"error"` and `"failed_node":null`.                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `load_rejects_empty_pin_with_no_form_and_pin_on_head_and_unknown_endpoint` | `check` and `serve` both refuse a scenario pinning `empty` on a no-form route, a `HEAD`, or an unknown endpoint, with the matching message; an `empty` pin on `GET /users` loads.                                                                                                                                        |
| `pins_only_scenario_activates_and_deactivates`                             | `PUT /scenarios/rt` with only `pins`: a 200 status on `error` is 400, an `empty` pin with no form is 400, a good one is 200 and `GET` shows the pin; activating makes `/users` 404, deactivating restores 200.                                                                                                           |
| `scenario_ttl_expires_its_pins`                                            | A scenario with a pin activated with `ttl: 300ms` answers 500 at first and 200 within three seconds.                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `states_empty_body_is_verbatim_not_param_substituted`                      | A `states.empty.body` of `{id: keep}` on `/users/{id}` is returned as written, not replaced by the path id.                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `pin_beats_wire_failures_and_a_downed_node`                                | With `reset` or `malformed` chaos on the app, `success` and `empty` pins answer a complete 200 JSON object (trace shows `app.error=reset                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `head_on_empty_and_loading_pins_has_no_body`                               | `HEAD` under an `empty` and a `loading` pin has the same status as the `GET`, `pinned=` in the trace and no body.                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `pinned_event_lists_the_chain_nodes_but_no_failure`                        | The event of a `success`-pinned request over a downed db lists `db` among its nodes, with `failed_node` and `kind` null.                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `built_in_route_needs_an_override_to_take_an_empty_pin`                    | `POST /login` of an auth node with no `override:` cannot be pinned `empty` ("no empty form"); with `override: { status: 204 }` it is an override route (a 204 has no body) and the pin loads.                                                                                                                            |
| `a_204_or_304_empty_pin_sends_no_body`                                     | A live `empty` pin with status 204 on a normally-200 route answers 204 with no body and no non-zero `content-length`; so does a route that normally answers 304, pinned `empty` through a scenario.                                                                                                                      |
| `put_get_delete_pins_round_trip`                                           | `PUT /pins` (lower-case method, 503, 150 ms) is 200; `GET` lists it under `live` and `effective` (source `live`, `delay_ms` 150); `/users/7` is 503; `DELETE ?endpoint=` is 204 (again too) and the pin is gone.                                                                                                         |
| `delete_without_query_clears_all_live_pins`                                | With two live pins and a scenario pin, `DELETE /pins` clears both live pins and leaves the scenario's in `effective`.                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `bad_pins_are_400_or_404_and_change_nothing`                               | Unknown endpoint 404; `HEAD`, malformed key, unknown state, `loading` with a status, a 6 min delay, an unparseable delay and `empty` with no form 400, on both `PUT /pins` and `POST /cycle`; nothing is pinned afterwards, and a failed write keeps the earlier pin.                                                    |
| `cycle_walks_the_five_states_and_wraps`                                    | Six `POST /cycle` calls return `loading, empty, error, slow, success, loading`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `cycle_skips_empty_on_a_built_in_route`                                    | On `POST /login` the cycle is `loading, error, slow, success, loading`, and asking for `empty` is 400.                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `cycle_with_state_pins_it_and_with_invalid_status_is_400`                  | `cycle` with `state: error, status: 429, delay: 1s` answers `{endpoint, state, status, delay_ms: 1000}` and `/users/7` is 429; `status: 200` on `error`, or a bare `status` that lands on `slow`, is 400 and the earlier pin stays.                                                                                      |
| `live_pin_masks_a_scenario_pin_and_off_restores_it`                        | A live `success` over an active 503 scenario pin answers 200 (source `live`); deleting it brings the 503 back (source the scenario).                                                                                                                                                                                     |
