| `demo_serves_the_storefront_and_leaves_only_dot_kaanal`            | `kaanal demo` prints the `#token=` URL, the seed and locale, how to stop and a story command; serves the page and `/products`; after `kaanal down` only `.kaanal/` is left. |
| `run_json_is_removed_after_stop`                                   | `--json` prints one object with `url`, `locale` and `seed` and without the token; `run.json` exists while running and is gone after stop.                                   |
| `a_second_demo_exits_64_and_leaves_the_files_alone`                | A second `demo` in the folder exits 64 "already running" and does not rewrite `.kaanal/demo/` (an edited file and the locale stay).                                         |
| `demo_next_to_a_kaanal_yaml_notes_it_and_works`                    | A broken `kaanal.yaml` in the folder is noted on stderr, left untouched, and the demo serves.                                                                               |
| `kaanal_chaos_from_the_same_folder_changes_the_demo`               | `kaanal chaos set nginx --down` from the folder turns `/products` into a 503.                                                                                               |
| `locale_flag_is_written_into_the_demo_config`                      | `--locale de-DE` replaces the config's `locale:` line and shows in the banner.                                                                                              |
| `an_unknown_locale_is_a_usage_error_64`                            | `xx-XX`, `../x` and `en-us` exit 64 listing the packs, and nothing is written.                                                                                              |
| `a_busy_default_port_falls_back_and_prints_the_real_url`           | With 8080 taken, the demo binds another port and prints it.                                                                                                                 |
| `demo_works_in_a_folder_with_spaces_and_non_ascii`                 | It serves and stops cleanly in such a folder.                                                                                                                               |
| `demo_has_no_host_or_detach_flag`                                  | `--host`, `-d`, `--detach`, `--detached` and `--wait` are usage errors that write nothing; help lists `--port`, `--locale` and `--open`.                                    |
| `eject_writes_the_project_and_starts_no_server`                    | `--eject` writes exactly the `FILES` with their text, returns, leaves no state beside the project, and the result validates.                                                |
| `the_ejected_project_has_a_peak_profile_over_its_own_endpoints`    | `traffic.peak` is 100 rps for 30 s with one journey per `endpoints:` key (GET 3, others 1); `validate` passes with no "not a route" warning.                                |
| `eject_refuses_a_non_empty_directory_without_force`                | A non-empty folder exits 1 naming `--force` and is left as it was.                                                                                                          |
| `eject_into_an_empty_existing_directory_works`                     | An existing empty folder receives the project.                                                                                                                              |
| `ejected_project_serves_the_same_storefront_under_plain_up`        | `kaanal up` in the ejected folder serves the page, six products, a cart round trip and the ten stories.                                                                     |
| `eject_writes_the_chosen_locale`                                   | `--locale de-DE` is written; no flag keeps en-US; an unknown tag exits 64 and creates nothing.                                                                              |
| `eject_prints_the_next_command`                                    | Text and `--json` output: the root, `cd DIR && kaanal up` (quoted for spaces), and the written files.                                                                       |
| `eject_while_a_demo_runs_works`                                    | `--eject` succeeds while a demo runs in the folder, which keeps serving.                                                                                                    |
| `force_without_eject_is_usage_error_64`                            | `--force` alone, and `--eject` with `--port` or `--open`, exit 64 and write nothing.                                                                                        |
| `eject_does_not_create_dot_kaanal`                                 | `--eject` leaves no `.kaanal/` in the working folder or the project.                                                                                                        |
| `eject_force_keeps_foreign_files_and_overwrites_template_files`    | With `--force`, the template's files (an edited `kaanal.yaml`) are rewritten and other files stay.                                                                          |
| `eject_through_a_linked_dir_is_refused`                            | A linked DIR exits 1 "symbolic link", with or without `--force`, and nothing is written through it.                                                                         |

## `crates/kaanal/tests/cli/` — binary end-to-end

Spawns the real compiled binary; `Up` starts `kaanal up --port 0` in a temp dir and kills it on drop. One test target (`cargo test -p kaanal --test cli`), one module per topic in `tests/cli/` (R3.8), with `main.rs` only declaring them:

| Module         | What it covers                                                                                                                                                       |
| -------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `common.rs`    | Shared helpers: the binary, scratch folders, the `Up` child process, and the helpers used by more than one module.                                                   |
| `validate.rs`  | `kaanal validate`: valid, cyclic and malformed configs; self-check lines for a response that breaks its schema, on `validate` and on `up` (after the port is bound). |
| `status.rs`    | `status` and `chaos` against a live instance, and stale run files.                                                                                                   |
| `scenario.rs`  | `scenario` commands: activate, load, save, reset and the `--json` client commands.                                                                                   |
| `startup.rs`   | Startup races and run-file permissions: killed instances, simultaneous `up`s, bad arguments.                                                                         |
| `project.rs`   | Whole-project checks: the sample project, the schema, the seed line, dictionaries and locales.                                                                       |
| `endpoints.rs` | `kaanal endpoints` and the endpoints file.                                                                                                                           |
| `keys.rs`      | The auth signing key: generation, reuse, corruption, permissions.                                                                                                    |
| `pins.rs`      | Scenario `ttl`, `cycle` and `pins` commands.                                                                                                                         |
| `logs.rs`      | `kaanal logs` and `trace`, including `--follow`.                                                                                                                     |
| `usage.rs`     | Exit code 64 for every kind of bad argument.                                                                                                                         |
| `lifecycle.rs` | Stale state, the startup window and the stop paths.                                                                                                                  |
| `down.rs`      | `kaanal down`.                                                                                                                                                       |
| `detached.rs`  | `kaanal up -d`.                                                                                                                                                      |
| `json.rs`      | The global `--json` switch.                                                                                                                                          |
| `remote.rs`    | `--endpoint` and `--token`, remote `down` and completions.                                                                                                           |
| `node.rs`      | `kaanal node down                                                                                                                                                    |

| Test                                                              | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `validate_places_errors_in_later_nodes_and_edges`                 | `validate` prints `file:line:col` for an unknown field in the second node and for the edge that closes a cycle.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `validate_ok`                                                     | Fixture config → exit 0.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `validate_cycle_fails_with_message`                               | A cycle config → exit 1 with "cycle" on stderr.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `validate_reports_generated_responses_that_break_their_schema`    | A spec whose `example` is over its own `maximum` exits 0 and prints `GET /orders: 5 of 5 generated samples violate the response schema: /total: maximum`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `up_serves_first_then_reports_schema_violations`                  | `up` writes `run.json` and answers `/__kaanal/health` before the same line appears on its stderr (the check runs on a thread after the banner).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `validate_bad_yaml_shows_file_line_col`                           | `kaanal.yaml:2:` prefix in the error (rule R6.1).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `validate_prints_unsupported_keyword_warnings`                    | Warnings on stderr, `ok:` on stdout, exit 0.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `status_without_running_instance_exits_2`                         | No `run.json` → exit 2 "not running".                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `stale_run_file_exits_2`                                          | A `run.json` pointing at a dead port → exit 2.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `live_status_chaos_and_errors`                                    | Full loop: status shows nodes; `chaos set` with all flags is reflected in status (`+300ms, 10% errors (503)`); `--kind hang` shows as `100% errors (hang)`; `chaos set --route "get /users/{id}" --down` (lowercase method) makes the GET return 503 and status shows an `endpoint GET /users/{id}:` line with DOWN, and `chaos clear --endpoint` returns it to 200; unknown node -> exit 1 with the server's message; an ANSI escape in a node name is neutralised to `?`; `chaos clear` removes all; a second `up` in the same dir -> "already running".                                                                                                                      |
| `chaos_set_without_any_setting_is_an_error_not_an_empty_patch`    | `chaos set db` alone: exit 64 "nothing to set", no empty patch sent.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `killed_instance_does_not_block_the_next_up`                      | After a kill -9 style death, the OS releases the lock; a new `up` starts and `status` works (rule R6.7).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `two_simultaneous_ups_let_exactly_one_win`                        | Race: exactly one instance exits 1, the winner keeps running.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `run_file_is_restricted_to_the_current_user_on_windows`           | (Windows only) `icacls` shows only the current user on `.kaanal/run.json` — no Everyone/Users grants (rule R5.8).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `cli_reaches_an_instance_bound_to_ipv6_loopback`                  | `up --host ::1` then `status` works (CLI connects to `run.json`'s host, sends `Host: localhost:<port>`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `bad_arguments_exit_64_not_2`                                     | Usage errors (`--error 70000`, unknown subcommand) exit 64, not clap's 2 ("not running"); `--help` exits 0.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `spec_text_cannot_drive_the_terminal`                             | An ESC sequence in a spec property name reaches the warning as `?[31m…`, never as a raw escape.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `sample_project_validates`                                        | `examples/nginx-server/kaanal.yaml` validates with no warnings.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `gatewayless_example_validates`                                   | `examples/gatewayless/kaanal.yaml` (service -> postgres database, no gateway) validates with no warnings.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `schema_prints_the_committed_schema`                              | `kaanal schema` exits 0 and prints JSON equal to `kaanal.schema.json`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `up_prints_the_realistic_seed`                                    | Under `determinism: realistic`, `up` prints `Seed:  N (determinism: realistic; replay its timings and failures with determinism: controlled and seed: N; generated data then follows that seed too)`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `validate_rejects_routes_with_a_pointer`                          | A file with `routes:` fails at the key (`kaanal.yaml:4:1: routes: was replaced by endpoints: (see docs)`), exit 1.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `endpoints_command_prints_and_appends_only`                       | `endpoints` prints the skeleton without writing; `--write` appends to an external file leaving old text untouched and keeping CRLF; a second run says "nothing to add"; `validate` then has no warnings.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `endpoints_write_refuses_inline_and_existing_files`               | An inline `endpoints:` map is print-only (kaanal.yaml unchanged, a note on stderr); with no `endpoints:` key `--write` creates `kaanal.endpoints.yaml` and names the line to add; it then refuses to touch that file while kaanal.yaml does not name it. Inline output is indented for pasting and validates once pasted; a flow-style file is refused with a hint.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `validate_warns_about_stale_entries_and_ignored_error_maps`       | `validate` warns about an entry no spec defines (and with no override) and about the `error_map` of a `via:` target, and still exits 0.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `validate_places_errors_in_the_endpoints_file`                    | A missing endpoints file is placed at the `endpoints:` value in kaanal.yaml; errors inside the file print as `kaanal.endpoints.yaml:line:col`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `live_scenarios_activate_deactivate_and_errors`                   | With an inline `db-down`: `scenario list` shows it as inline and not active; `activate` makes the list say `(active)`, requests 503 and `status` show DOWN and `active scenarios: db-down`; `show` prints `(inline)` and the definition; `deactivate` prints "deactivated db-down" (then "is not active" the second time) and requests are 200; `scenario set` with no names empties the stack; unknown name -> exit 1 with 404 "unknown scenario"; `show active/save/reset` -> "reserved" (exit 64); loading a file named like the inline scenario -> 409.                                                                                                                     |
| `scenario_load_places_shape_errors_in_the_file_and_sends_nothing` | Nine bad files (rate 2, bad kind, duplicate key, empty `chaos: []` ("at least one of chaos or pins"), status 404, 999 999 ms latency, nothing set, no target, endpoint without a method) each exit 1 with `file:line:col` and the expected message words; afterwards none of them is in `scenario list` and the active stack is empty.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `status_shows_each_failure_band_at_its_own_share`                 | Two scenarios on one node (30% 503 over 30% reset, activated via `scenario set a b`) print `30% errors (503), 21% errors (reset)`, never the cumulative 51%.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `scenario_load_checks_the_file_here`                              | A good file loads and activates (`scenario active` names it, `status` shows `+5ms`); a misspelled field fails as `typo.yaml:2:`; a file named `Bad Name.yaml` fails with "invalid scenario name" (exit 64); a file with `limits:` fails with "not supported" and does not appear in `scenario list` (a `pins:` file is now accepted).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `scenario_save_and_reset`                                         | `chaos set db --latency 7ms` then `scenario save mine` writes `scenarios/mine.yaml` with only `latency_ms: 7` for db (no `error_rate` or `down`); a second save fails "already exists"; `--force` replaces it with the new 9 ms; `--from mine` (bare name) is refused with a hint at `scenario:NAME` (exit 64); `--clear-live` moves the live 9 ms into scenario `moved` (gone from status until activated); `--from scenario:mine` copies the file content; `--from effective` writes `eff.yaml` and, after `scenario clear`, loading it again restores the same status rows; `reset` empties the stack, undefines the loaded (runtime) `eff2` and keeps `mine` and `db-down`. |
| `status_shows_endpoint_rows_and_chaos_warns`                      | `chaos set db --kind hang` (no rate) prints a `warning:` on stderr and still exits 0; `status` then shows `endpoint KEY: +9ms` and `db @ KEY: +11ms` rows.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `up_generates_the_key_once_then_reuses_it`                        | The one key generation by kaanal itself: prints `Generating signing key...` and the `Auth:` line, writes one key file (no temp; 0700/0600 on Unix), and the next run serves the same `kid` without generating.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `up_uses_a_pre_seeded_key`                                        | A key already at the expected path is used as is (the served `kid` is its `kid`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `corrupt_key_file_stops_startup_naming_it`                        | `up` exits 1 naming the file and the delete hint; the file is untouched.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `two_simultaneous_ups_share_one_key`                              | Two `up` of one project from different working directories both start, serve the same `kid`, and leave exactly one `.pem` and no temp file.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `up_with_a_broken_config_generates_no_key`                        | `up` on an auth config with a topology error exits 1 with that error, prints no `Generating signing key...` and does not create the key directory.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `validate_never_touches_keys_and_warns_without_users`             | `validate` of an auth config with no users warns and never creates the key directory.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `endpoints_command_skips_built_in_routes`                         | `kaanal endpoints` lists only spec routes: the built-in login and JWKS are never in the skeleton.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `unix_key_files_readable_by_others_are_refused`                   | (Unix only) A mode-0644 key stops `up` with a `chmod 600` hint, and the keys directory is made 0700 anyway.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `explicit_wide_grants_in_the_keys_dir_are_removed_on_windows`     | (Windows only) A keys directory and key file that were explicitly granted to Everyone before `up` lose that grant.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `keys_dir_is_restricted_to_the_current_user_on_windows`           | (Windows only) `icacls` on the keys directory shows the current user and none of Everyone, Users, Authenticated Users.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `activate_with_ttl_lists_and_expires`                             | `scenario activate db-down --ttl 300ms` prints `active scenarios: db-down`; `scenario list` shows `(active, expires in ...` (or already not active on a slow box) and, within 3 s, no longer shows `(active`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `bad_ttl_is_rejected_here`                                        | `--ttl 5` (no unit) fails in the CLI with `bad ttl` (exit 64) and nothing is activated.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `active_stays_names_only`                                         | After `activate --ttl 30s`, `scenario active` prints just `db-down`, while `scenario list` shows `(active, expires in`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

### Data files and locales (D1)

| Test                                                                       | What it proves                                                                                                                                            |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `validate_reports_a_bad_dictionary_entry_with_line_and_column`             | A `data.yaml` with `gen: sku_code` exits 1 with `data.yaml:3:` and a message listing the valid generators.                                                |
| `validate_accepts_the_ja_jp_fixture_project_and_rejects_an_unknown_locale` | The fixture project (a `ja-JP` pack in `locales/`) validates; `locale: ja-JP` without the pack exits 1 at `kaanal.yaml:9:9` listing `en-IN, en-US`.       |
| `schema_prints_config_dictionary_or_pack`                                  | `kaanal schema` equals `schema config`; `schema dictionary` and `schema pack` print JSON Schemas with `fields` and `first_names`; `schema nope` exits 64. |

### Pins

| Test                                                            | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| --------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `cycle_steps_and_prints_the_state`                              | Six `kaanal cycle "GET /users"` calls print `GET /users -> loading`, `empty`, `error`, `slow`, `success`, `loading`.                                                                                                                                                      |
| `cycle_state_flag_and_off`                                      | `--state error --status 404 --delay 2s` prints `GET /users -> error (status 404, delay 2s)`; `--off` prints `-> unpinned` and `kaanal pins` says `no pins` (also on a second `--off`); a braced key survives the query encoding; `--off` with `--state` is a usage error. |
| `cycle_bad_state_rejected_here`                                 | A bad `--state` lists the valid names and a bad `--delay` mentions delay, both exit 64, before anything is sent (no pin appears).                                                                                                                                         |
| `cycle_server_400_is_shown_and_changes_nothing`                 | A first step with `--status 404` lands on `loading`: the CLI shows `HTTP 400` and nothing is pinned.                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `pins_lists_live_and_effective_with_source`                     | `kaanal pins` prints `no pins`, then (live `slow` plus a loaded scenario pin) the exact `live pins:` and `effective pins:` blocks with `live` and `scenario:pinned (status 404)`, and `no live pins` when only the scenario pins.                                         |
| `status_shows_pins`                                             | `status` has no `pin` line at first, then `pin GET /users: error (live)`.                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `scenario_show_prints_pins`                                     | `scenario show` of a loaded pins-only file prints `pin GET /users: error` with `status 404` and `delay 2s`.                                                                                                                                                               |
| `scenario_load_accepts_pins_only_file_and_still_refuses_limits` | A pins-only file loads and activates; a pin with a status on `slow` fails as `file:line:col` and is not defined; a file with `limits:` is still refused ("not supported").                                                                                                |

### Logs and trace

`hit` sends a mock request (optionally with a client `X-Request-Id`) and returns the status and the `X-Request-Id` it got; `Follow` runs `kaanal logs -f` and reads its stdout lines on a channel.

| Test                                                               | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `logs_default_n_and_each_filter`                                   | Three `/health`, a `/nope` and a chaos `503` on `GET /users/{id}`: `logs` prints 5 lines including `GET /health  200`; `-n 2` prints 2; `--status 404` is the single `unmatched` line; `--status 404,5xx` 2; `--failed` is the `/users/1` line with `failed=`; `--route "GET /users/{id}"` the `503`; `--scope nobody` prints nothing; `--status 9xx` and `--scope ""` fail naming the flag (exit 64). |
| `logs_since_and_json_lines`                                        | `logs --json` prints one JSON object per line with `client_id` and a `hops` array; `--since 1h` keeps both; after a pause `--since 10ms` prints nothing; `--since yesterday` fails naming `since` (exit 64).                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `trace_last_by_id_and_by_client_id`                                | `trace last` is the latest request; `trace <id>` and `trace my-req-9` (the client id) both print the same waterfall, starting with the id and carrying `client=my-req-9`, bars (`#`, `\|`) and `db.read`; `--json` has the id.                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `trace_evicted_unknown_and_exit_codes`                             | On an empty ring `trace last` says `no requests recorded yet`; after `reset` the old id fails with `404`, `evicted` and `older than`; `r0000-0001` and an unsent client id fail with `unknown`; `bad id!` fails with `400`; `trace last` and `logs -f` with no running instance exit 2.                                                                                                                |
| `logs_follow_prints_tail_then_new_requests_without_duplicates`     | `-f -n 2` prints the last two old requests, then (with an 800 ms chaos on one route) a slow request that started first and a fast one: the fast one is printed first, the slow one when it finishes, and no extra line follows.                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `logs_follow_survives_a_request_finishing_between_tail_and_stream` | 60 requests 5 ms apart while a follower starts mid-way with `-n 1000`: it prints each client id exactly once (no duplicate, no gap) and nothing more afterwards.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `logs_rejects_n_outside_1_to_1000_and_a_closed_pipe_is_quiet`      | `-n 0` and `-n 1001` fail (exit 64) naming `-n` and 1000; `-n 1000` works; `logs` and `trace last` with the reader already gone exit 0 with empty stderr.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `logs_follow_filters_endpoint_failed_and_json_live`                | A follower with `--route "GET /users/{id}"` prints the two matching tail lines, then only the matching live ones (no `/health`); a follower with `--failed --json` prints full JSON entries (with `hops` and `endpoint`) only for failed requests, live as well as in the tail.                                                                                                                        |
| `logs_follow_exits_2_on_down`                                      | `kaanal down` under a running `logs -f` ends the follower with exit code 2 and `kaanal stopped (the server shut down)` on stderr (the `shutdown` event, then the lock going away).                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `logs_follow_exits_2_when_the_server_dies`                         | Killing the server under a running `logs -f` ends it with exit code 2 (the lock is free once the process is gone).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |

### Exit codes (usage errors are 64)

`assert_64` runs the CLI in an empty scratch directory with no instance (so the failure can only come from the arguments, found before any request) and asserts exit 64; `usage_fails` does the same against a running `Up`.

| Test                                                        | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ----------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `bad_log_filter_exits_64_without_an_instance_in_both_modes` | `logs` and `logs -f` with `--status 9xx`, `--since yesterday` or `--scope ""` all exit 64 with no instance running: the filter is checked before the instance is looked up, so both modes agree. |
| `bad_names_in_set_and_save_exit_64`                         | `scenario set ok "Bad Name"` and `scenario save "Bad Name"` exit 64.                                                                                                                             |
| `unknown_flag_exits_64`                                     | `status --bogus` exits 64 (clap's usage error, remapped from 2).                                                                                                                                 |
| `unknown_subcommand_exits_64`                               | `frobnicate` exits 64.                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `missing_required_arg_exits_64`                             | `trace` with no key and `chaos set` with no node or endpoint exit 64.                                                                                                                            |
| `conflicting_flags_exit_64`                                 | `cycle "GET /x" --off --state error` exits 64.                                                                                                                                                   |
| `help_and_version_exit_0`                                   | `--help`, `-V`, `--version`, `help chaos` and `chaos set --help` exit 0.                                                                                                                         |
| `bare_command_without_subcommand_exits_64`                  | `chaos` alone and no arguments at all exit 64.                                                                                                                                                   |
| `bad_ttl_exits_64`                                          | `scenario activate db-down --ttl 5` exits 64.                                                                                                                                                    |
| `bad_scenario_name_exits_64`                                | `scenario show active` (reserved) and `scenario deactivate "Bad Name"` exit 64.                                                                                                                  |
| `bad_state_exits_64`                                        | `cycle "GET /x" --state broken` exits 64.                                                                                                                                                        |
| `bad_delay_exits_64`                                        | `cycle "GET /x" --delay soon` exits 64.                                                                                                                                                          |
| `bad_from_exits_64`                                         | `scenario save x --from mine` (a bare name) exits 64.                                                                                                                                            |
| `n_out_of_range_exits_64`                                   | `logs -n 0` and `logs -n 1001` exit 64.                                                                                                                                                          |
| `empty_scope_exits_64`                                      | `logs --scope ""` exits 64.                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `chaos_set_nothing_exits_64`                                | `chaos set db` with no setting exits 64.                                                                                                                                                         |
| `bad_latency_exits_64`                                      | `chaos set db --latency 15` (no unit) exits 64.                                                                                                                                                  |
| `bad_rate_exits_64`                                         | `chaos set db --error-rate 150%` exits 64.                                                                                                                                                       |
| `scenario_load_bad_file_name_exits_64`                      | `scenario load "Bad Name.yaml"` exits 64.                                                                                                                                                        |
| `a_server_400_is_still_exit_1`                              | `trace "bad id!"` parses here and is refused by the server: stderr says `HTTP 400` and the exit code is 1, not 64.                                                                               |
| `bad_timeout_is_usage_64`                                   | `down --timeout` with `soon`, `5` (no unit), `0s` and `301s` exits 64.                                                                                                                           |
| `wait_zero_is_usage_64`                                     | `up -d --wait 0s` exits 64 and creates no `.kaanal` directory.                                                                                                                                   |

### Stale state and the startup window

| Test                                                             | What it proves                                                                                                                                                       |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `up_writes_a_token_free_startup_object_to_run_json`              | `run.json` has a `startup` object with a `warnings` array, and the token appears nowhere inside it.                                                                  |
| `post_shutdown_stops_a_foreground_up_and_removes_run_json`       | A `POST /__kaanal/shutdown` with no body answers 2xx; the foreground `up` exits 0 within 20 s and `run.json` is gone.                                                |
| `a_killed_up_leaves_stale_state_that_status_reports_and_removes` | After a hard kill the `run.json` is still there; `status` exits 2 with "stale state" and the file is gone afterwards.                                                |
| `up_over_stale_state_starts_normally`                            | A planted `run.json` (port 9, token `stale`, pid 1) with no lock is replaced: a new `up` writes a different token and `status` exits 0.                              |
| `detached_flag_prints_ui_url_without_token`                      | `up --detached` prints a `UI:` line that ends in `/__kaanal/ui` and contains no `token`.                                                                             |
| `foreground_up_keeps_the_token_in_the_ui_url`                    | Plain `up` prints `/__kaanal/ui#token=`.                                                                                                                             |
| `held_lock_without_run_json_is_starting_exit_1`                  | A lock held by the test and no `run.json`: `status` exits 1 with "starting or its state file" (after the 5 s window), not 2.                                         |
| `held_lock_with_an_unanswering_api_is_exit_1_not_2`              | A held lock, a `run.json` pointing at a listener that accepts and drops every connection: `status` exits 1 with "did not answer", and the `run.json` is not deleted. |

### `kaanal down`

`down_in` runs `kaanal down` in a directory; `Helper` kills and reaps a helper process on drop.

| Test                                                  | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ----------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `down_stops_a_running_up_and_status_then_exits_2`     | `down` prints `stopped` and exits 0; the foreground `up` exits 0 within 20 s, `run.json` is gone and `status` exits 2.                                                                                                                                         |
| `down_twice_exits_0_both_times`                       | Two `down`s in a row exit 0, and a third prints "not running".                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `down_with_nothing_running_exits_0`                   | In an empty directory `down` prints exactly `kaanal is not running` and exits 0.                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `down_removes_stale_state_and_exits_0`                | After a hard kill, `down` exits 0 and prints `removed stale state from pid N` for the dead pid; `run.json` is gone.                                                                                                                                            |
| `down_kills_after_a_wrong_token`                      | With a wrong token in `run.json` the shutdown call is a 401; `down --timeout 10s` goes straight to the kill (under 8 s, not 12), exits 0 and prints exactly `stopped (killed pid N after 10s)`; neither stream contains the real token; `status` then exits 2. |
| `down_refuses_to_kill_a_non_kaanal_pid`               | A `run.json` naming a helper process (`ping` on Windows, `sleep` elsewhere) under a held lock and a closed port: `down --timeout 1s` exits 1 saying `pid N` is `not a kaanal process`, and the helper is still alive.                                          |
| `down_cuts_a_hanging_request_at_the_budget`           | With 30 s latency on every node and a request in flight, `down --timeout 1s` takes under 4 s, exits 0 and prints `stopped`; the client's request ends.                                                                                                         |
| `concurrent_downs_both_succeed`                       | Two `down`s started together both exit 0 and neither panics.                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `down_during_startup_window_waits_then_fails_clearly` | A held lock with no `run.json`: `down` waits at least 4 s, then exits 1 saying it is "starting".                                                                                                                                                               |
| `sigterm_stops_up_gracefully`                         | (Unix only) 20 rounds of `kill -TERM` the moment `run.json` appears: every round exits 0 and removes `run.json`, which proves the handlers were registered before anything was published.                                                                      |

### `kaanal up -d`

`piped` runs a command with both output pipes read to EOF and fails if EOF does not come within 30 s (a daemon that inherited the pipes would hold them open); `Daemon` stops whatever lives in a directory on drop.

| Test                                                            | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| --------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `piped_up_d_returns_while_the_daemon_keeps_running`             | The pipe test: with stdout and stderr piped, both reach EOF within 20 s while the daemon keeps running; the command exits 0, `status` exits 0 and `down` exits 0.                                               |
| `up_d_returns_after_health_and_run_json_has_a_live_port`        | Exit 0; the stdout has the `#token=` of `run.json`, a `Log:` line and `Stop: kaanal down`; `run.json`'s port equals the `Mocks:` port and `/__kaanal/health` answers 200.                                       |
| `up_d_log_exists_and_holds_no_token`                            | `.kaanal/kaanal.log` has the `Mocks:` line and neither the token nor `token=`.                                                                                                                                  |
| `second_up_d_exits_0_with_already_running_and_the_same_port`    | A second `up -d` exits 0, prints the same `Mocks:` port and `already running (pid N)` for the pid in `run.json`.                                                                                                |
| `up_d_then_down_then_status_exits_2`                            | `up -d`, `down` (0), then `status` exits 2.                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `up_d_rotates_the_previous_log`                                 | After `up -d`, `down`, `up -d`, `kaanal.log.1` holds exactly the first run's log.                                                                                                                               |
| `up_d_with_a_bad_config_exits_1_and_spawns_nothing`             | A config that parses but has a cycle: exit 1 with `kaanal.yaml` and "cycle" on stderr, and no `.kaanal` directory exists afterwards.                                                                            |
| `up_d_with_a_missing_config_exits_1`                            | A missing file: exit 1 naming `nope.yaml`, no `.kaanal` directory.                                                                                                                                              |
| `up_d_when_the_port_search_is_exhausted_exits_1_with_log_lines` | With 21 consecutive ports held, the child cannot bind: exit 1 and the stderr carries the log's "no free port".                                                                                                  |
| `up_d_prints_auth_and_seed_lines`                               | With an auth node and `determinism: realistic`, the parent prints `Auth:  POST http://localhost:` (`1 user`) and `Seed:  ... determinism: realistic`, which only the child's `run.json` `startup` could supply. |
| `up_d_works_in_a_path_with_spaces_and_non_ascii`                | In a directory named `upd späce 日本` `up -d` exits 0 and health answers 200.                                                                                                                                   |
| `simultaneous_up_d_starts_exactly_one_instance`                 | Five `up -d` at once all exit 0 with one port; exactly four print `already running`; `status` works; the live log still holds the `Mocks:` line and no `kaanal.log.1` was made.                                 |
| `up_d_that_times_out_leaves_no_process_behind`                  | `--wait 1ms` on an auth config (slow to start): exit 1 with "no answer within"; the instance lock becomes free within 3 s (nothing is orphaned) and `status` exits 2.                                           |
| `up_d_warns_about_a_non_loopback_host_in_the_parent`            | `--host 0.0.0.0` prints the `warning: binding 0.0.0.0 ...` line exactly once on the parent's stderr, with the "reachable from your network" text.                                                               |

Every CLI test with an auth node sets `KAANAL_CONFIG_DIR` to a scratch directory and pre-seeds one shared key, except the one keygen test.

### JSON, remote mode and completions

`remote` runs the binary in a directory with extra environment variables; `port_and_token` reads the control port and token from a running instance's `run.json`; `free_port` finds a port nothing listens on. A few tests stand up a tiny TCP listener that answers like a stuck or silent kaanal.

| Test                                                               | What it proves                                                                                                                                                               |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `every_client_command_honours_json`                                | Every client command (`status`, `pins`, every `scenario` form, `chaos set                                                                                                    |
| `json_flag_works_before_and_after_the_subcommand`                  | `status --json` and `--json status` give one line, an array of rows with `node` and `type`.                                                                                  |
| `json_env_switches_it_on_and_empty_env_is_off`                     | `KAANAL_JSON=1` and `true` switch it on; an empty value leaves the text table (`NODE ...`).                                                                                  |
| `json_pins_and_scenario_reads_parse`                               | `pins`, `scenario list`, `scenario active` and `scenario show` each print one JSON value of the expected shape (`chaos` is an array in `show`).                              |
| `logs_and_trace_json_output_is_unchanged`                          | `--json logs` and `logs --json` print the same bytes (the `client_id` is there); `--json trace ID` prints the entry with that `id`.                                          |
| `json_mode_keeps_errors_on_stderr`                                 | With nothing running, `status --json` exits 2, stdout is empty and stderr says "not running".                                                                                |
| `endpoint_reaches_an_instance_from_another_directory`              | `--endpoint` plus `KAANAL_TOKEN` (and the `KAANAL_ENDPOINT` form) from an unrelated directory works, creates no `.kaanal`, and warns about nothing for a loopback address.   |
| `endpoint_flag_may_follow_the_subcommand`                          | `status --endpoint URL` works; after `chaos set`, `logs` and `chaos clear` a URL still means the instance (not a route key).                                                 |
| `endpoint_without_a_token_is_usage_error_64`                       | An endpoint with no token is 64 "needs a token"; an empty `KAANAL_TOKEN` counts as unset.                                                                                    |
| `token_flag_without_endpoint_is_64_but_env_token_alone_is_ignored` | `--token x` and `--token ""` without an endpoint are 64 naming `--endpoint`; an exported `KAANAL_TOKEN` alone, or an empty `KAANAL_ENDPOINT`, leaves local commands working. |
| `https_endpoint_is_64`                                             | `https://`, a path, no scheme, no port are all 64; the https message says "plain http"; a password in the URL is refused and never echoed.                                   |
| `up_with_an_endpoint_is_64`                                        | `up` with `--endpoint` is 64 naming the flag; `up` and `up -d` with `KAANAL_ENDPOINT` set are 64 naming the variable; nothing is created.                                    |
| `remote_refused_is_exit_1_not_2`                                   | A port nothing listens on: `status` and `logs -f` exit 1 with `cannot reach URL`, and the token is not in the message.                                                       |
| `wrong_token_is_exit_1_token_rejected`                             | A wrong token (`status` and `logs -f`) is exit 1 `token rejected`, never echoing the token.                                                                                  |
| `non_loopback_endpoint_warns_once_on_stderr_only`                  | An unroutable address prints `warning: sending the token over plain http to ...` exactly once, on stderr, without the token; exit 1.                                         |
| `unresolvable_host_is_exit_1`                                      | A host name that does not resolve is exit 1 `cannot reach`.                                                                                                                  |
| `local_down_ignores_an_unrelated_token_env`                        | A local `down` with `KAANAL_TOKEN` exported still works as a local stop (`stopped`, `run.json` gone).                                                                        |
| `remote_down_stops_the_instance_without_touching_pids`             | `--endpoint URL down` from another directory prints `stopped`, exit 0, creates no `.kaanal`, and the instance process ends on its own.                                       |
| `remote_down_when_nothing_answers_is_exit_1`                       | `down` against a dead port is exit 1 `cannot reach`, not 0 and not 2.                                                                                                        |
| `remote_down_that_never_stops_is_exit_1_and_does_not_kill`         | A listener that answers 202 to the shutdown and 200 to health forever: exit 1 "still answering ... not killing".                                                             |
| `remote_down_with_a_silent_health_is_exit_1_not_stopped`           | A listener that accepts connections but never answers health: exit 1, empty stdout (no false `stopped`), within 5 s.                                                         |
| `completions_print_a_script_for_every_shell`                       | `completions bash                                                                                                                                                            |
| `completions_need_no_instance_and_ignore_json`                     | In an empty directory `completions bash` and `--json completions bash` print identical output and create no `.kaanal`.                                                       |
| `completions_unknown_shell_is_64`                                  | `completions tcsh` is a usage error, 64.                                                                                                                                     |

### `node`, `--for` and `node list` (C2b Part 2)

Same file, same harness; the timed ones sleep past a 1 s lease.

| Test                                                         | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `node_down_for_reverts_by_itself`                            | `node down db --for 3s` prints `(reverts in 3s)`, `status` shows DOWN, and about 3.7 s later it does not: the server reverted it, no CLI process involved.                          |
| `node_up_clears_only_the_down_flag`                          | `chaos set db --latency 50ms`, `node down db`, `node up db`: DOWN is gone and `+50ms` stays.                                                                                        |
| `node_up_is_a_noop_exit_0_when_nothing_is_down`              | `node up db` on a healthy node exits 0.                                                                                                                                             |
| `node_up_exits_1_when_a_scenario_holds_it_down`              | With a `down: true` scenario active, `node up db` is exit 1 `db is still down: held by an active scenario or override`, naming `kaanal scenario active`.                            |
| `node_down_unknown_node_is_exit_1`                           | `node down nope` is exit 1 with the server's `unknown node 'nope'`.                                                                                                                 |
