| `an_edge_is_set_and_cleared_with_from_colon_to`              | (`node.rs`) `chaos set --edge gw:app` with latency, jitter and a cap names the link `gw -> app`, shows in `--json`, and `chaos clear --edge` removes it.                            |
| `status_lists_an_edge_entry_with_its_jitter_and_cap`         | `kaanal status` prints `gw -> app: +20ms ±5ms, cap 500000 B/s` while the entry is set and nothing after `chaos clear --edge`.                                                       |
| `a_replica_goes_down_and_up_and_is_named_as_node_hash_index` | (`node.rs`) `node down app --replica 1 --for 30s` prints `app#1: down (reverts in 30s)`; `chaos set --replica`, `node up --replica` (`app#1: up`) and `chaos clear --replica` work. |
| `a_replica_out_of_range_is_exit_1_with_the_range`            | `--replica 9` on a node with 3 replicas exits 1 with `node 'app' has 3 replicas (0 to 2)`, for `node down` and `chaos clear`.                                                       |
| `replica_needs_a_node_and_excludes_route`                    | `chaos set --replica 1` with no node, and with `--route`, are usage errors (64).                                                                                                    |
| `node_list_shows_the_replica_count_of_a_replicated_node`     | (`node.rs`) The table shows `app x3` and no `gw x`; `--json` rows carry `replicas: 3` for app only.                                                                                 |
| `chaos_set_for_sends_a_ttl_and_a_bad_value_is_64`            | `chaos set db --down --for 30s` ends `(reverts in 30s)`; `--for 3s` leases and reverts; `5`, `soon`, `50ms` and `25h` are 64 `bad --for` on both `chaos set` and `node down`.       |
| `completions_mention_node`                                   | The bash completion script contains `node`.                                                                                                                                         |
| `node_list_matches_the_status_table`                         | `node list` prints exactly the node table at the top of `status` (header with `STATE` included).                                                                                    |
| `node_list_shows_state_and_the_revert_countdown`             | After `node down db --for 30s`, the `db` row has `down` in the STATE column and `reverts in`.                                                                                       |
| `node_list_json_is_an_array_with_state`                      | `node list --json` is an array; the `db` row has `state: "down"` and a positive `expires_in_ms`.                                                                                    |

## `crates/kaanal/src/instance/lifecycle/tests.rs` (unit) — liveness, stopping and detaching

Every function takes the state directory, so these use a scratch directory per test (`dir`, removed on drop); `hold` stands in for a running instance by holding the exclusive lock, `write_run` writes a minimal `run.json` (port 4000, pid 77). See [lifecycle](files/kaanal/lifecycle.md).

| Test                                                                     | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `lock_probe_is_free_without_a_lock_file`                                 | No lock file means nobody is running.                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `lock_probe_is_running_while_another_handle_holds_it`                    | A held exclusive lock reads as `Running`.                                                                                                                                                              |
| `lock_probe_is_free_after_the_holder_drops`                              | Dropping the holder (what process death does) makes it `Free`.                                                                                                                                         |
| `lock_probe_reports_an_error_instead_of_free`                            | When `lock` is a directory (unusable), the probe is an exit-1 error, never "stale".                                                                                                                    |
| `a_free_probe_holds_the_shared_lock_until_dropped`                       | A `Free` probe keeps its shared guard (an exclusive try fails) until it is dropped, then the exclusive lock can be taken.                                                                              |
| `read_run_wait_sees_a_file_that_appears_late`                            | A `run.json` written 250 ms later is read within the budget.                                                                                                                                           |
| `read_run_wait_gives_up_after_the_budget`                                | No file: `None` after the short budget.                                                                                                                                                                |
| `run_json_without_startup_still_parses`                                  | A file with no `startup` and no `host` parses (host `127.0.0.1`, default `Startup`); one with `startup` gives seed 5 and the warnings.                                                                 |
| `remove_stale_keeps_a_file_with_a_different_token`                       | The file is deleted only for the token that was read.                                                                                                                                                  |
| `require_instance_covers_every_row`                                      | Free + file: exit 2 "removed stale state", file gone. Free + none: exit 2 "no .kaanal/run.json". Held + file: the `Run`, file untouched. Held + none: exit 1 "starting or its state file is missing".  |
| `unreadable_run_json_with_a_free_lock_is_removed_and_reported`           | A corrupt file under a free lock: exit 2 "removed unreadable state", file deleted.                                                                                                                     |
| `run_json_without_a_lock_file_is_stale_and_guarded`                      | A `run.json` with no lock file is stale (exit 2), and a lock file is created so the delete is guarded.                                                                                                 |
| `transport_failure_removes_stale_state_when_the_lock_is_free`            | A transport error with a free lock is exit 2 and the stale file goes.                                                                                                                                  |
| `transport_failure_uses_the_lock`                                        | Free lock: exit 2 "not running on 127.0.0.1:4000: refused". Held lock: exit 1 "running (pid 77)" with the reason.                                                                                      |
| `clear_stale_reports_each_row_and_leaves_a_held_lock_alone`              | Nothing: "kaanal is not running". File: "removed stale state from pid 77", file gone. Held lock: `None` and the file stays.                                                                            |
| `wait_lock_free_times_out_while_held_and_sees_a_release`                 | While held, false after the full 300 ms; released by a thread after 200 ms, true.                                                                                                                      |
| `run_or_gone_turns_a_vanished_instance_into_gone`                        | A lock released after 250 ms gives `Found::Gone`; a lock that stays held with no file is an error.                                                                                                     |
| `parse_tasklist_csv_reads_the_image_name`                                | `"kaanal.exe",...` and `"ping.exe",...` give the name; the "INFO: No tasks" line and empty output give `None`.                                                                                         |
| `parse_ps_comm_trims_and_takes_the_basename`                             | `kaanal`, `sleep`, `/usr/local/bin/kaanal` give the base name; a blank line gives `None`.                                                                                                              |
| `verify_pid_accepts_this_executable_and_the_standard_name_only`          | The test process itself is `IsKaanal` (its stem is `current_exe`'s). `KAANAL.EXE`, an own stem in any case: yes; `kaanal-1a2b.exe` against own stem `kaanal`, `ping.exe`: no.                          |
| `a_truncated_comm_matches_a_longer_own_stem_by_prefix`                   | With the Linux 15-character limit a 23-character own stem matches its first 15 characters (any case) and not a different prefix or a shorter one; without the limit the truncated name does not match. |
| `image_names_with_multibyte_text_do_not_panic`                           | Names such as `日本語` and `a日本語.exe` are compared without a panic; `日本語.EXE` matches an own stem `日本語`.                                                                                      |
| `a_failing_lock_probe_is_an_error_not_a_stop`                            | `force_stop` with an unusable lock is exit 1, and neither `verify` nor `kill` is called.                                                                                                               |
| `force_stop_refuses_to_stop_itself`                                      | `pid == me` is exit 1 "refusing to stop myself", before any verify or kill.                                                                                                                            |
| `force_stop_reports_stopped_when_the_instance_vanishes_before_verifying` | `verify` fails ("tasklist failed") but the lock is gone by then: `Stopped`, no kill.                                                                                                                   |
| `force_stop_refuses_unknown_and_other_while_the_lock_is_held`            | `Unknown` and `Other` verdicts with the lock held are errors and never reach the kill.                                                                                                                 |
| `force_stop_does_not_kill_when_the_token_changes_before_the_kill`        | A new instance replaces `run.json` between verify and kill: exit 1 "changed while stopping", the kill closure is never called.                                                                         |
| `force_stop_kills_a_verified_unchanged_instance`                         | `IsKaanal` and an unchanged `run.json`: `kill(77)` is called, the lock drops, the result is `Killed` and `run.json` is gone.                                                                           |
| `settle_unready_keeps_a_healthy_child_and_otherwise_always_ends_it`      | Healthy: `Healthy`, no shutdown, no kill. Not healthy with its own `run.json`: shutdown, wait and kill all run. Not healthy without one: only the kill.                                                |
| `wait_ready_times_out_with_a_fake_poll`                                  | `poll_until` returns `TimedOut` after a few polls, `ChildExited` at once for an exited child, and the `Run` when a step turns ready.                                                                   |
| `rotate_log_replaces_the_old_backup`                                     | `kaanal.log` ("second") becomes `kaanal.log.1` over the old backup ("first") and a fresh empty log is made; with no previous log there is just a fresh one.                                            |
| `rotate_log_truncates_when_the_old_log_cannot_be_renamed`                | (Windows only) A log held open without delete-sharing cannot be renamed: the new log is empty and no `.1` is made.                                                                                     |
| `tail_log_returns_the_last_n_cleaned_lines`                              | Control bytes become `?` and invalid UTF-8 U+FFFD in the last 3 of 4 lines; asking for more returns all; a missing file gives an empty string.                                                         |

## `crates/kaanal/src/util/logview/tests.rs` (unit) — lines, waterfalls and follow

Golden strings; no network. See [logview](files/kaanal/logview.md).

| Test                                                        | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ----------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `line_formats_flags_and_utc_time`                           | `45296789` ms renders `12:34:56.789Z  r04f2-0042  GET /orders/7  200  112ms`; with scope, pin, failed node, kind, client id and `rejected` set the suffixes come in that order.                                                                  |
| `waterfall_success`                                         | Two hops (`nginx.proc` 0 to 10 ms, `db.read` 10 to 40 ms of 40) print the exact header, labels, start and duration columns and 40-column bars (`#` in columns 0 to 10 and 10 to 40).                                                             |
| `waterfall_queue_wait`                                      | A hop that waited 10 ms draws spaces, then ten `.`, then ten `#`.                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `waterfall_chaos_latency_why`                               | The header ends `trace 40.0ms  flags=DI` and the row ends `\|  chaos +30ms`.                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `waterfall_rate_limited_429`                                | A 429 hop of zero duration still draws an `x`, and the footer is `failed at gw: 429 (rate-limit)`.                                                                                                                                               |
| `waterfall_hang_timeout_cut_hop`                            | A cut hop is drawn with 35 `x`, its `chaos hang` note, and the footer `failed at db: 504 timeout (deadline)`.                                                                                                                                    |
| `waterfall_names_the_replica_of_a_replicated_hop`           | A hop with `replica: 2` prints `db#2.op`; a hop without one prints `app.proc` with no `#`.                                                                                                                                                       |
| `waterfall_pinned_over_failure_footer`                      | A pin over a chain failure puts `pinned=empty` in the header, ends `pinned=empty hid: db.error=503`, and still marks the failed hop.                                                                                                             |
| `waterfall_parallel_lanes`                                  | Two hops in lanes 1 and 2 overlap in time on their own rows, noted `lane 1` and `lane 2`.                                                                                                                                                        |
| `waterfall_tiny_hop_gets_one_column`                        | A 0.1 ms hop of a 1000 ms trace draws exactly one `#`; a zero-length hop at the very end still fits the bar.                                                                                                                                     |
| `waterfall_truncated_entry_and_hopless_entries`             | A truncated entry ends `... more hops (truncated, 1 shown)`; a `client-gone` entry shows `- (client-gone)`; an entry with none shows `- (no hops recorded)`.                                                                                     |
| `terminal_escapes_in_names_are_neutralised`                 | An ESC sequence in the id, method, path, scope, client id, kind, rejected, pin, failed node, flags, hop node, kind, op, note and chain-error fields (and the lagged notice) leaves no control character in the output; each field shows `?[31m`. |
| `client_side_filters_match_the_server_grammar`              | `status`, `failed`, `scope`, `endpoint` and `since` judge an entry as the server would; a value without `endpoint` (an unmatched request) cannot pass an endpoint filter; a bad status is an error from `filter_of`.                             |
| `lagged_notice_text`                                        | `-- missed 17 requests (follow slower than the server) --`.                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `seen_is_bounded_and_dedups`                                | A repeated id is refused; after 2000 newer ids the oldest is forgotten, the newest is not.                                                                                                                                                       |
| `sse_lines_assemble_into_messages`                          | Keep-alive comments and blank lines produce nothing; `event:`/`data:` lines (with or without a space) produce `request` and `lagged` messages.                                                                                                   |
| `sse_bytes_that_are_not_utf8_do_not_end_the_reader`         | Invalid bytes become U+FFFD and the message still completes.                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `a_full_queue_counts_drops_and_the_printer_takes_the_count` | A queue of 2 fed 5 requests counts 3 drops; a `scenario` message that does not fit counts none; taking the count resets it; a gone printer makes `forward` return false.                                                                         |
| `a_server_lagged_message_that_does_not_fit_adds_its_count`  | Two requests that do not fit count 2, a `lagged` message of `17\n` adds 17, junk adds 1: total 19.                                                                                                                                               |
| `drops_are_still_there_after_the_stream_ends`               | The count survives the end marker and is taken after it.                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `drain_flushes_pending_drops_at_the_end_marker`             | The printer prints `-- missed 2 requests ... --` and returns `Marker` when the end marker arrives.                                                                                                                                               |
| `drain_flushes_pending_drops_when_the_queue_is_cut`         | The same when the reader dies without a marker: `Disconnected`.                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `drain_flushes_pending_drops_while_idle`                    | With an idle wait of 20 ms the notice is out before any message or marker; later a request goes to `on_event` and a server `lagged` prints its own notice.                                                                                       |

## `crates/kaanal-server/src/actor.rs` (unit) — the live engine task

| Test                                                                | What it proves                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `the_actor_gives_the_same_records_as_the_engine_driven_directly`    | 20 plans submitted 1 ms apart through the actor (paused clock) give the same arrival, done, result and hops as the engine driven directly.                                                                                                                                             |
| `an_arrival_at_the_release_instant_overflows_like_the_simulator`    | A plan submitted at the exact instant a no-queue slot frees overflows (503), the same tie order as the engine and the simulator.                                                                                                                                                       |
| `a_dropped_receiver_does_not_stop_the_engine`                       | Aborting a waiting handler leaves the engine running; the next request still completes.                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `the_refill_runs_already_due_events_before_it_refills`              | Paused clock: a hop already due when `refill_buckets` arrives takes its token from the old bucket, so the next request finds a full one (verified: without the `advance_to` it got 429).                                                                                               |
| `simultaneous_arrivals_beat_an_already_due_release_like_the_engine` | Paused clock: three plans sent at one instant (`join!`) to a zero-duration `{ limit: 1, queue: 1 }` gateway give the engine's answers (ok, queued ok, 503): an already-due release never jumps a queued arrival (verified: an `advance_to` before reading the channel gave three oks). |
| `a_lone_zero_duration_request_has_no_timer_floor`                   | Real clock: the median of 50 sequential zero-duration requests is under 10 ms, so an already-due event does not wait for a timer tick (verified: with `sleep_until` alone the median was 15.9 ms on Windows; elsewhere the tick is 1 ms, so it only guards Windows).                   |
| `actor_throughput` _(ignored benchmark)_                            | `cargo test -p kaanal-server --release --lib -- --ignored --nocapture actor_throughput`: requests per second through the actor for a limit-free zero-duration plan.                                                                                                                    |

chaos leases on the same timer: `chaos_ttl_expires_and_clears_only_its_unit` (permanent latency 200 plus `down` for 30 s: at 31 s `down` is gone, the latency stays, and Control, the snapshot and the effective chaos agree), `expiry_removes_an_entry_left_empty`, `a_24h_scenario_lease_does_not_delay_a_1s_chaos_lease` (the task asleep on a 24 h scenario lease is woken by the chaos PUT and fires at 1 s; the scenario stays), `a_reput_just_before_the_deadline_wins` (re-PUT at 29 s: still down at 31 s, `expire_due` does nothing, gone at the new deadline), `plain_put_just_before_the_deadline_makes_it_permanent`, `expiry_after_the_entry_was_deleted_emits_nothing_and_does_not_spin` (no event, `next_deadline` is `None`; a stale lease with no entry is dropped silently too), `expire_event_names_the_chaos_key` (`expire`, `name: None`, `chaos_key` `{"node":"db"}`, and for an endpoint and a node@endpoint key `{"endpoint":"GET /x"}` and `{"node":"db","endpoint":"GET /x"}`; the SSE data carries `chaos_key` only when set), `two_units_of_one_key_expiring_together_publish_once_and_emit_once` (two keys, three units: `expire_due` returns 2, one event per key), `failed_expiry_drops_the_lease_and_keeps_the_chaos` (an out-of-range entry stored without a publish makes the recompile fail: the chaos stays, no event, no lease left to spin on)., `a_due_chaos_lease_is_dropped_even_with_no_generation` (with no generation a past-due chaos lease is dropped with no event, so the lease task does not spin), `a_stale_generation_is_refused_under_the_control_lock` (`lock_gen` accepts the generation in force and answers 409 for one an Apply replaced), `locked_pairs_the_lock_with_the_generation_in_force_and_never_refuses_a_swap` (`locked` returns the generation in force after a swap, and 409 only with none) |

## `crates/kaanal-sim/tests/sim/` — simulator engine

One test target (`cargo test -p kaanal-sim --test sim`), one module per topic in `tests/sim/` (R3.8):

| Module            | What it covers                                                                                                                                                         |
| ----------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `common.rs`       | Shared helpers and the compared configs (`tests/common/mod.rs` is included as `shared`).                                                                               |
| `zero_load.rs`    | Zero-load equality against `execute` and live.                                                                                                                         |
| `behaviour.rs`    | Lindley, FIFO, determinism, overload, utilization, warmup.                                                                                                             |
| `config.rs`       | Config validation, counters, absurd values, bounded queues.                                                                                                            |
| `streams.rs`      | Streams named by compiled endpoints, the hop budget.                                                                                                                   |
| `forks.rs`        | Fork and join: ties, cancelled siblings, slots.                                                                                                                        |
| `auth.rs`         | Login and JWKS as stream keys.                                                                                                                                         |
| `hold.rs`         | `hold: call` end to end: held app busy, report agrees with engine, no-slot node unchanged.                                                                             |
| `summary.rs` (F7) | `unit_text_and_integer_bytes_give_the_same_summary`: `250KB/s` and `256000` give the identical summary (links, pipes, p99).                                            |
| `defaults.rs`     | F3: a 16-slot app at 1.3x capacity, unbounded (p99 far past 30 s, 0 failed) against `queue: 100` with a 30 s gateway timeout (turns the excess away, p99 within 30 s). |

| Test                                                                      | What it pins                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `zero_load_equals_live_exactly`                                           | Fixture, sample, a gateway-less topology, the `CHAINS` config (every step type), a `replicas` config (every policy, a weighted edge override) and a `prob` config (edge defaults, a step override, a call inside `parallel`), with and without 50% errors on `db`/`pay`/`c`: hops, latency and result equal `execute()` for the same `n`; no waits; failures inside `parallel` occur (> 100 cut).                                |
| `engine_matches_lindley_recursion`                                        | Single-slot M/M/1 at ρ = 0.8: every wait equals Lindley's recursion exactly, in ns; more than 1 000 requests queued.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `simultaneous_events_keep_fifo_order`                                     | 1e6 req/s with an 800 ns service: hundreds of arrivals tie at the same nanosecond, yet every wait still equals the Lindley recursion (FIFO under ties).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `hand_edited_absurd_service_time_is_rejected_not_a_panic`                 | A built `Topology` edited to a `Duration::MAX` service time (or exponential mean) returns `Err` naming the node and "10h", not a panic (the same guard catches a YAML `p50` above `p99`, which core turns into an uncapped fixed `p50`).                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `clock_overflow_is_an_error_not_a_silently_wrong_report`                  | 9 h fixed service, one slot, 600 000 arrivals: `run` returns `Err` containing "clock overflow" instead of pinning the clock.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `same_seed_same_report`                                                   | Fixed service times: identical reports on a re-run; seed 43 differs.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `down_gateway_completes_with_no_hops`                                     | Zero-hop chains complete at arrival with 503.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `overload_grows_the_queue_but_finishes`                                   | ρ = 1.5: max queue > 500, utilization in 0.95–1.0 (clipped to the window), every request completes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `unlimited_nodes_report_zero_utilization_and_no_queue`                    | A node without `concurrency` reports 0 utilization and an empty queue.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `warmup_requests_are_not_reported`                                        | Requests before `warmup` are dropped, but counters keep running through it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `request_counters_start_at_zero_and_run_on_consecutively`                 | Each stream's `n` is 0, 1, 2 … as in live mode.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `utilization_divides_by_the_slot_count`                                   | 2 slots at 1.0 busy slot on average → utilization ≈ 0.5 (after warmup).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `absurd_chaos_latency_is_rejected_not_a_panic`                            | Same limits as live mode (R6.9): `latency_add` of exactly 5 min runs; 5 min + 1 ms and `error_status: 418` return `Err` naming the field instead of overflowing the clock.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `streams_name_compiled_endpoints`                                         | Any compiled endpoint may be a stream, a static one included (one hop, 1 ms); an unknown key is `stream 0: unknown endpoint 'GET /nope'`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `hop_budget_counts_chain_length`                                          | R6.13: a chain of 20 201 hops at 1 req/s for 1 000 s is rejected with the exact "workload too large" message (arrivals × hops against 20 M).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `cancelled_siblings_keep_their_slot_but_queued_ones_leave`                | A parallel branch fails at 5 ms: the running sibling keeps its one slot to 10 ms (record cut at 5 ms, utilization counts the full 10 ms), the queued sibling leaves its queue and never runs.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `hop_budget_sums_streams_and_skips_warmup`                                | R6.13: two streams each under 20 M records but 24.2 M together are rejected; a warmup counts only the reported arrivals (1 080, not 1 200) against that budget, but warmup arrivals still count against the 50 M total-work cap.                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `zero_cost_failure_ties_go_to_the_first_branch`                           | D17: two failing branches tie at 0, one through a zero-cost hop; `execute()` and the engine (simulator and live) both fail at the lower branch's node.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `fork_join_ends_with_the_slowest_branch`                                  | `parallel: [a 3 ms, s 4 ms]` takes 4 ms.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `one_slot_serializes_parallel_branches`                                   | Three branches on a one-slot node run FIFO in branch order, waiting 0, 4, 8 ms; latency 12 ms.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `failure_ties_go_to_the_lowest_branch_like_live`                          | Two branches fail at the same instant: the lower plan index wins (D17), and hops and latency equal live's.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `branch_waits_under_load`                                                 | R7.6: a `parallel` block on a one-slot node always queues its second branch, so the queueing branch is exercised by forks.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `login_is_a_stream_key_and_auth_concurrency_queues_logins`                | `POST /login` is a stream key with no simulator change (spec section 6): at 50 req/s on a one-slot auth node (10 ms mean) every login succeeds, reads the user database (X12) and queues at the auth node.                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `login_and_jwks_equal_live_at_zero_load`                                  | `POST /login` and the JWKS streamed on three topologies (gateway edge with `calls: 2`, no edge, auth only), with and without 30% chaos on auth's dependencies: every request's hops, latency and result equal the live `execute` for the same `n`, with no queueing; the flaky runs do fail logins.                                                                                                                              |
| `rejects_bad_config`                                                      | Each validation rule: no streams, bad rates (0, NaN, 1e7, 1e-7), unknown endpoint, zero duration, warmup ≥ duration, > 365 days, duplicate keys, workload too large.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `too_many_streams_are_rejected_not_truncated`                             | 65 536 streams: `run` returns `Err` containing "at most 65535 streams" (a request tag packs the stream index into 16 bits).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `bounded_queue_and_rate_limit_counts_match_the_failed_requests`           | 120/s into an 80 rps limit feeding a one-slot node with a queue of 2: `limited` equals the 429 count, `overflowed` equals the 503 count, `max_queue` never exceeds 2, and every 429 carries `retry_after`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `sim_reports_timeouts_per_node_and_errors_by_kind`                        | `NodeStats::timed_out` counts deadlines at the span's node and `SimReport::errors_by_kind` splits failures into `status`, `reset`, `malformed`, `timeout`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `zero_load_equals_execute_for_every_kind`                                 | R7.5 drift guard: at 1 req/s every outcome kind (status, reset, hang, malformed) matches the independent `execute` record for record.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `zero_load_equals_execute_with_spans`                                     | Same guard with `timeout:` spans, fired and not.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `zero_load_equals_execute_for_a_hang_under_spans`                         | Same guard for a hang under an enclosing span.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `zero_load_equals_execute_for_a_deadline_in_a_parallel_branch`            | Same guard for a deadline inside a `parallel` branch (cut siblings).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `zero_load_equals_execute_for_endpoint_chaos`                             | Same guard for endpoint-wide chaos (`Delay` hop, offset-0 failure).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `zero_load_equals_execute_for_a_scenario_stack`                           | R7.5 drift guard for B2b-1: two stacked scenarios plus a live +3 ms layer compiled once, at 1 req/s: each of the four kinds (status, reset, hang as timeout, malformed) fires and matches `execute`; a banded 30% 503 / 30% reset yields both kinds; a node@endpoint entry, an endpoint entry with latency, and a live `replace` that calms db (no errors at all) all equal `execute` record for record through `run_effective`. |
| `replicated_node_utilization_is_per_slot_of_every_replica`                | (`behaviour.rs`) Two replicas of one slot at 100 req/s of 10 ms work report utilization 0.5, not 1.0.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `zero_load_equals_execute_with_replicas_and_replica_chaos`                | (`replicas.rs`) A config with replicas on gateway, app (random) and db (round-robin, weighted edge): the sim equals `execute` hop for hop, replica included, calm and with one db replica down and one app replica slow; more than one replica is really used.                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `a_downed_replica_never_serves_in_the_simulator`                          | With db replica 1 down no hop lands on it, while the other replicas serve.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `link_chain_matches_execute`                                              | (`links.rs`) Streams with request and response sizes over fast bandwidth edges and a `per_kb` node: every record equals `execute` at zero load, with both link directions planned.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `link_chaos_plan_matches_the_simulator`                                   | (`links.rs`) Latency, jitter, a cap and a 20% drop rate on a link: every record equals `execute`, and some requests are dropped.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `partition_under_a_span_cancels_like_a_hang`                              | A partitioned edge to a node with a 200 ms timeout: every request is a 504, equal to `execute`; the planned failure wins the tie so `timed_out` stays 0.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `an_inline_auth_route_on_a_replicated_gateway_runs_without_a_bad_replica` | (`replicas.rs`) 50 req/s of `POST /login` through a 3-replica gateway with inline auth: no panic, every auth hop is replica 0 and auth completes its hops.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `visits_match_sim_completed_counts`                                       | (`visits.rs`) 200 000 simulated requests over a chain with a cache, `repeat`, `prob` and a `parallel` block: each node's completed hops per request match `expected_visits` within 5%.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |

`tests/common/mod.rs` holds the `CHAINS` config, its `CHAIN_ROUTES` and `compiled(yaml, routes)`, shared by `sim.rs` and `distribution.rs`.

## `crates/kaanal-core/tests/replay/` — replay engine

| Test                                                                             | What it pins                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `fifo_hand_over_serves_waiters_in_arrival_order`                                 | Three 10 ms hops through one slot, arriving at 0, 1 and 2 ms: waits 0, 9 and 18 ms, answers at 10, 20 and 30 ms.                                                                                                                                                    |
| `arrival_before_the_release_event_of_the_same_instant_queues_behind_waiters`     | A request arriving at the instant a slot is released (before that event is stepped) waits behind the queue: starts at 0, 10 and 20 ms, no barging.                                                                                                                  |
| `answer_is_emitted_at_the_failure_instant`                                       | A failing branch answers at 5 ms although a sibling hop runs until 10 ms: the running hop is cut at the failure, still holds its slot, and nothing is emitted twice.                                                                                                |
| `stamp_orders_arrivals_with_events`                                              | `stamp()`, `peek()` and `next_due()` share one `(time, seq)` order, so a caller's own queue merges with the engine's.                                                                                                                                               |
| `late_advance_gives_the_same_records_as_stepping_on_time`                        | One `advance_to` far in the future gives the same records as stepping event by event: events run at their scheduled times, not at the call.                                                                                                                         |
| `clock_overflow_is_an_error`                                                     | A hop ending past `u64::MAX` ns returns `EngineError::ClockOverflow` from `submit`, and `step` keeps failing.                                                                                                                                                       |
| `the_request_table_is_reused`                                                    | 100 sequential requests use one table slot and are all counted.                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `submit_runs_earlier_events_itself_and_keeps_same_instant_ties`                  | `submit` without `advance_to` first runs the events due strictly before `now` (release at 10 ms hands the slot to the waiter), so a late caller gets exact waits (9 ms, 5 ms) and answers (30 ms).                                                                  |
| `submitting_into_the_past_is_a_clear_panic`                                      | `submit` earlier than the engine clock panics with "earlier than the engine clock", not a late arithmetic panic.                                                                                                                                                    |
| `drain_finished_iter_yields_the_same_answers_and_empties_the_buffer`             | The capacity-keeping drain returns the same answers as `drain_finished` and leaves nothing behind.                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `an_empty_bucket_fails_the_request_with_429_and_retry_after_and_takes_no_slot`   | Rate limit 1 rps, burst 1: the second request at 1 ms fails at once with 429 and `retry_after` 999 ms, starts no hop, takes no slot and does not queue (`limited` 1).                                                                                               |
| `a_full_queue_fails_with_the_gate_status`                                        | Pool of 1 with a queue of 1 and overflow status 504: the third of three simultaneous requests fails at 0 ms with 504 and no `retry_after` (`overflowed` 1, `max_queue` 1); the second waits 10 ms.                                                                  |
| `release_instant_arrival_does_not_barge_with_a_bounded_queue`                    | An arrival at the instant a slot is released, before that event runs, still finds the bounded queue full (503) and the waiter keeps its place.                                                                                                                      |
| `a_visit_takes_one_token_however_many_own_hops_it_has`                           | A monolith's proc and inline op in one visit take one token (burst 1 is enough).                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `each_call_of_a_node_is_its_own_visit`                                           | Three calls (net+proc each) to a node with burst 2: the third visit is refused (429) after its net hop, five hops recorded.                                                                                                                                         |
| `parallel_branches_each_take_a_token`                                            | Two branches calling a burst-1 node: the second branch's visit finds the bucket empty (429).                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `a_chaos_failure_takes_no_token`                                                 | A planned chaos failure at a rate-limited node spends no token, so the next request passes.                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `an_engine_failure_cancels_queued_siblings_like_a_planned_one`                   | A 429 in one branch removes the sibling queued at another node: that hop never runs (`completed` 1).                                                                                                                                                                |
| `a_planned_failure_that_replaces_a_refusal_takes_its_count_back`                 | A 429 refused first at 3 ms is replaced by a lower-index planned `Fail` at the same instant (D17): the answer is the planned 500 with no `retry_after`, and `limited` is 0 (found by review: it was 1). The replaced refusal leaves cause `Chaos`, not `RateLimit`. |
| `an_own_hop_after_a_join_starts_a_new_visit`                                     | An own hop at a node after a `parallel` block (whose branch ran an edge hop) is a new visit and takes a token: with burst 1 the second visit is refused (429).                                                                                                      |
| `zombie_hop_keeps_the_slot_and_waiters_time_out_in_the_queue`                    | Z4 trace: 500 ms timeout, 400 ms ops, one slot, three simultaneous requests. #1 finishes at 400, #2 times out running at 500 (zombie until 800), #3 times out in the queue at 500.                                                                                  |
| `finishing_exactly_at_the_deadline_is_ok`                                        | A hop that ends at the deadline instant succeeds: deadlines run in a later phase of the instant (Z14).                                                                                                                                                              |
| `a_planned_fail_at_the_deadline_instant_beats_the_deadline`                      | A planned `Fail` reached at the deadline instant is decided first, at a lower or higher plan index alike.                                                                                                                                                           |
| `span_ending_in_a_parallel_block_stays_open`                                     | A span that ends in a `parallel:` stays open until the join, so a branch overrun still times the request out (review B3).                                                                                                                                           |
| `stale_deadline_never_hits_a_recycled_request_slot`                              | A leftover deadline of a finished request does not fire on a later request that reuses the table slot (review B2).                                                                                                                                                  |
| `closed_span_ignores_its_deadline`                                               | A span whose body finished in time is closed; its deadline pops later and does nothing.                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `nested_spans_outer_wins_at_equal_deadlines`                                     | Equal deadlines on nested spans: the outer (lower plan index) span fires and its node is reported.                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `deadline_inside_a_parallel_branch_cancels_the_siblings`                         | A deadline in one branch fails the request and removes queued siblings; running ones keep their slots. The failure's cause is `Deadline`.                                                                                                                           |
| `equal_deadlines_in_two_branches_fail_at_the_lower_plan_index`                   | Two branch deadlines at one instant: the lower plan index fires.                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `a_deadline_at_the_hand_over_instant_leaves_a_zombie_that_the_client_never_sees` | A deadline at the instant a slot is handed over: the hand-over runs, the request is answered, the handed-over hop is a zombie.                                                                                                                                      |
| `a_deadline_over_a_delay_hop_cuts_it`                                            | A deadline during an endpoint `Delay` hop answers 504 at the deadline.                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `a_deadline_with_one_sibling_queued_and_another_running`                         | Counters and slot state when the deadline finds one branch queued and another running.                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `timed_out_is_not_counted_for_a_pre_warmup_arrival`                              | `NodeCounters.timed_out` only counts requests arriving at or after the warm-up.                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `delay_hop_takes_no_slot_and_no_token`                                           | A `Delay` hop holds no gate slot and spends no rate-limit token (Z18).                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `engine_matches_the_reference_queue_with_patience`                               | R7.6: the engine equals an independent reference of one FIFO server with constant patience (waiters leave at their deadline, a started customer runs to the end), per customer: outcome and answer time.                                                            |
| `cause_is_none_on_success`                                                       | A request that succeeds has `Finished.cause == None`.                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `cause_chaos_for_a_planned_failure_and_a_hang`                                   | A planned `Fail` with status 503 and a planned `Timeout` (a hang) both finish with `FailCause::Chaos`.                                                                                                                                                              |
| `cause_rate_limit_for_a_429_from_the_bucket`                                     | Two requests at one instant through a node with a one-token bucket: the first has no cause, the second `RateLimit`.                                                                                                                                                 |
| `cause_overflow_for_a_full_queue`                                                | A pool of one slot and no queue: the second simultaneous request fails with `Overflow`.                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `cause_deadline_for_a_fired_timeout`                                             | A 100 ms `timeout:` span over a 400 ms hop fails with `Deadline`.                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `transfers_on_one_edge_queue_fifo`                                               | (`links.rs`) Three 10 ms transfers on one bandwidth edge end at 10, 20 and 30 ms; the third waited 20 ms; `link_counters` shows 3 started, queue 2, wait 30 ms, busy 30 ms.                                                                                         |
| `each_replica_has_its_own_slots`                                                 | (`replicas.rs`) With `concurrency: 1` and 2 replicas, hops on different replicas run at once (both done at 10 ms); two on one replica queue (10 and 20 ms, 10 ms wait).                                                                                             |
| `rate_limit_is_per_replica`                                                      | A `burst: 1` bucket gives each replica one token: one request each succeeds, a second on one replica gets a 429.                                                                                                                                                    |
| `node_counters_sum_the_replicas_and_replica_counters_split_them`                 | Five hops (3 on replica 0, 2 on replica 1): node `completed` 5, per replica 3 and 2; node `max_queue` is the longest replica queue; node `wait` is the sum.                                                                                                         |
| `busy_time_sums_over_the_replicas`                                               | Two replicas busy 10 ms each give `area` 10 ms each and 20 ms for the node.                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `a_failed_request_leaves_the_queue_of_its_replica`                               | A branch queued on replica 1 is cancelled by a sibling's failure; a later request finds replica 1 free (no ghost waiter) and `max_queue` of replica 1 was 1.                                                                                                        |

| `cause_does_not_change_any_existing_outcome` | The fixtures of the 429 and deadline tests, rerun with the same expectations (result, `done` time, hop counts and cut durations) plus the cause: adding the field changed nothing else. |

## `crates/kaanal-sim/tests/theory.rs` — queueing theory (NFR-11)

| Test                                                             | What it pins                                                                                                                                                               |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `mm1_matches_theory`                                             | Single slot, exponential 10 ms, ρ = 0.5 (~200k requests) and 0.8 (~600k, as high load is autocorrelated): mean latency within 5% of 1/(μ − λ).                             |
| `mmc_matches_erlang_c`                                           | 3 slots, ρ = 0.7, ~400k requests: mean wait within 5% of Erlang C.                                                                                                         |
| `md1_matches_pollaczek_khinchine`                                | Fixed 10 ms, ρ = 0.7, ~400k requests: mean wait within 5% of ρ/(2μ(1 − ρ)).                                                                                                |
| `a_bandwidth_link_is_an_md1_queue`                               | 1000 B over 100 kB/s at 70 req/s (ρ = 0.7): the mean wait on the link matches Pollaczek-Khinchine within 5% (measured 1.37% off).                                          |
| `tandem_matches_jackson`                                         | app μ = 100 → db μ = 200, λ = 50: latency within 5% of 1/(μ₁ − λ) + 1/(μ₂ − λ).                                                                                            |
| `gatewayless_mm1_matches_theory`                                 | No gateway node, one slot, exponential 10 ms, ρ = 0.5: mean sojourn within 5% of 1/(μ−λ); queueing does not depend on a gateway (R1.23).                                   |
| `two_way_fork_join_matches_nelson_tantawi`                       | `parallel` over two identical M/M/1 servers, ρ = 0.5, ~400k requests: mean sojourn within 5% of (12 − ρ)/8 × 1/(μ − λ) (Flatto–Hahn, Nelson–Tantawi, K = 2).               |
| `replicated_node_under_random_routing_is_independent_mm1_queues` | Three random replicas at a total 180 req/s are three M/M/1 queues at 60 req/s: the mean wait is within 5% of rho/(mu - lambda) = 15 ms (measured 1.5% off, 600k requests). |
| `round_robin_waits_less_than_random_at_the_same_load`            | At the same load round-robin waits under 85% of random (measured 8.1 ms against 14.7 ms): Erlang-3 gaps are less bursty than Poisson ones.                                 |
| `a_pool_without_a_queue_matches_erlang_b`                        | `concurrency: { limit: 3, queue: 0 }`, offered load 2.4: the 503 share equals Erlang B blocking within 5% (measured 0.12% off).                                            |
| `a_bounded_queue_matches_mmck_blocking`                          | `{ limit: 2, queue: 3 }`, load 1.8: the 503 share equals M/M/2/5 blocking within 5% (0.32% off).                                                                           |
| `overflow_status_is_the_configured_one`                          | `overflow_status: 502` yields 502 failures and no 503.                                                                                                                     |
| `a_rate_limit_under_overload_passes_exactly_its_rps`             | Poisson 300/s into 100 rps with burst 50: the accepted rate is 100/s within 5% (0.00% off).                                                                                |

| `zero_load_matches_live_distribution` | Fixture, sample and `CHAINS`, `GET /users/{id}`: per `(node, kind)` (cache hits and repeats change the hop count; a kind common on one side must exist on the other) the two-sample KS statistic is below the α = 0.001 critical value against live `execute()` samples for n ≥ 1 000 000. |

## `crates/kaanal/tests/bench.rs` — benchmark suite (NFR-05)

One ignored test, `suite`, in the `kaanal` crate because it drives every crate. Run it in release: `cargo test -p kaanal --release --test bench -- --ignored --nocapture` (about 30 s). Each row runs 5 times and reports median, min and max; the table goes to stdout and JSON to `target/bench.json` (or `KAANAL_BENCH_OUT`). `KAANAL_BENCH_COMPARE=<file>` adds the % change of each median against a saved run. Baselines stay local; nothing fails on a regression.

| Section   | What it measures                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| --------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `core`    | `Topology::build`, chain `compile`, and `execute` / `sample_plan` per call, on the NFR-05 topology.                                                                                                                               |
| `sim`     | `one_million_requests`: the NFR-05 measurement (1M simulated requests through `kaanal_sim::run`), moved here from `kaanal-sim/tests/bench.rs` unchanged. The suite's only hard check: each run under 10 s and > 990 000 requests. |
| `data`    | `generate` over a small, medium (`User`) and large (100 orders) schema with default `Options` and with another built-in locale pack; `DataSet::load` of a project with a pack and `data.yaml`; built-in dictionary lookups.       |
| `server`  | The server in-process on an ephemeral port (as the server tests start it), zero-latency hops, one sequential keep-alive client: requests/s for a generated body, then with `error_rate: 0.5` chaos on `db`.                       |
| `overall` | The same server with 8 concurrent clients: requests/s and p50/p95/p99 latency.                                                                                                                                                    |

The `server` and `overall` rows go over loopback HTTP and are noisy: compare them only on the same machine.

### Running it: local script and CI

- **Locally:** `scripts/bench.sh [--runs N] [--save-baseline] [--compare FILE] [--out FILE]`. It runs the suite in release, keeps every run in `target/bench/` (ignored by git), merges `--runs N` runs (median of the medians), and compares with `target/bench/baseline.json` when one exists. Typical use: `scripts/bench.sh --runs 3 --save-baseline` before a change, `scripts/bench.sh --runs 3` after it.
- **CI, every push and pull request:** the `bench` job in `ci.yml` runs the suite three times and writes a comparison table into the job summary. A push to `main` stores its result as the `bench-baseline` artifact; later runs fetch the latest one to compare. It is report-only: a regression never fails the build, only the NFR-05 assertion can.
- **CI, manual cumulative report:** the `Benchmark report` workflow (Actions tab, Run workflow; inputs `runs` and `note`) benchmarks the chosen ref, adds an entry to a history kept as the `bench-history` artifact and publishes a table of the last eight entries in the job summary and as the `bench-report` artifact. Artifacts expire after 90 days, so run it now and then; the history restarts if the last artifact is gone.
- **Reading the numbers:** shared runners and busy laptops swing by 2x between runs, so changes within +-25% are marked `~`. Judge trends over several runs, not one comparison. `scripts/bench-report.mjs` (Node, no dependencies) does the merge, compare, append and report steps.
